JACKSON (Jane)
Book of medical receipts
(Dated) 1642








Jane Jacksone her Booke: written in the
yeere 1642








40969
A very shorte and compendious
Method of Physicke and Chirurgery
Containinge the Cures inwardly and
also the Cureing of all manner of
Woundes on the bodie together with
remedies for the stone and the causes
and signes thereof xc:
As alsoe
The making of Sirrups and all manner
of oyles and waters whatsover







Waights A Briefe declaration of the notes of all & eache kindes of weights tonic have  generallie used and contained throughout  this whole booke and the quaintities of  them

For as much as the true and perfect knowledg in compounding of medicines  cannot be attained unto without the knowledg of the quantities of waightes which is at this present day most comonly used in makeing of medicines I will therefore heere briefily declarenthe use of them for seeing that the waight of some medicines be soe instly and certainly ordeined and made that if any man doe chance rashlie to erre in the true quantitie thereof hee doth much hurte to the body and many times putteth the life in jeopardy you must therefore diligently endeavoure and applie your mind to studie and consider of the medicines, that soe you may have the more full and perfect knoledge thereof the notes bee these followinge.   1 |    Grana |         G
--- | -------- | --------- | ----

  2 | Scrupuli |         9 |
  3 |  Drachma |         3 |
  4 |    Uncia |         ℥ |
  5 |    Quar: |        gr |
  6 |    Libra | have this |   tb
  7 |   Semis: |     marke |  18:
  8 | Manipuli |           |    m
  9 |   Pugili |           |   p.
 10 |      Ana |           | ana.


  A scruple is 20 barly cornes 3 scuples containe a drame 8 dramms containe an ounce. Quart signifieth a quart of anything Libra is a pound Semiss is the halfe of every waight Manipulis is a greate handfull Pugilis is a small hafith Ana signifieth of every one like much





The manner of purging and prepareing the humours of the body

First of the blood There bee 4 humours in euery mans body which may bee putrified within the veines and without except blood which is putrifieth onely  within the veines therefore diseases that come of blood are holpen onely by letting of blood

1
The Prepareinge and purging of Choler.
A preparatiue.

Take Syrupe of Endiue the Syrupe of roses, opezaccharum Compound
of each i/8. water of Endiue of Chichorie and of  purclane of each them
ij medle them well together and give the sicke to drinke iij mornings
in the spring of day whether it bee in winter or summer a little
warmed .. on the fyer.

A Laxatiue

Take of Cassia new drawne  of the Electuarie of the iuyce
of roses ij of Diaprunis Laxative i desolue all these in Endiue water
and parsley water i/8 give th sicke to drinke an hower before the
day spring a little warmed ....on the fyer and let the sicke keepe
his bedd an hower after without any sleepe then lett him rise and
walke in his Chamber the windowes fast shutt keeping himselfe
warme from the ayer and winde all that day and after that hee
hath had 2. or 3. stooles giue him to drinke the broth of a Chicken
halfe sodden without salt if it bee a flesh day, if it bee any other day
giue him Almon milke or a draught of ptisane and then as the day
requireth ordaine for the patient meate that is wholesome and somwhat
according to his appetite And beware of all contrary meates as beefe
porke, all manner of venison hot spices and hott herbes as onyons
garlick porret and such herbes.

Another preparatiue for Choller

Take syrup of Agresta acct of simplicis of Byzan of each i/8. mingle
them together and give to the sick to drink 3 mornings a little
warmed.... as aforesd.

Another Laxatiue for Choller

Take Renbark grossly bruysed ij  Spicknard ij put them together
to steepe one night in Endiue water or white wine iiij.  In the
morning rub it well with your hande and streine it hard through
a cloath , put into the Liquor soe streined, electuary de succo rosarum





1
iii diacathelici ii and giue the sicke in the morning warme an
hower before day, all precepts kept as is shewed in the former
Laxatiue.

Pills to purger Choller

Take pilularp aurearp stomachicarp de chabarbara of cac/  j
medle them together with a little Syrupe of Endiue and
make it in 5 pills giue it the sicke at xj of the Clocke at
night.

Confortatiues after the purging of the Choller and
when the patient is thirsty and  dry and hott

Giue the patient all these conserves following or some of them,
mingled together Conserve of violetts of walter lillies of roses
of sorrell of barberies of Prunes,/

The preparing and purging of phlegme
A preparatiue

Take the Syrupe of Mints of Hyssope and of wormwoode
of each j . water of sauge of rew of Hysope of each ij
medle them well together and giue the patient to drink dram
in mornings a little wormwood as foresaid

A Laxative

Take Turbith well clensed within and without j of Garlicke
j ffine ginger ij salgme gu 5. lett these bee steeped together
a whole night in water of mints and Hysope of each ij
in the morning wring them hard through a cloath and -
pur to that Liquor of Diaphanicon ℥ iij of elect.m Indi
maioris ʒj ℥ij medle them together and giue the drinke
an hower before day a little wormwood and all other things
observed as before written.

Another preparatiue

Take the  ????? syrupe of Liquerice of fumetarie of Horhound of
each ℥j water of Broome Mints scabions of Elecampane of
each ℥ij medle them all together and giue to the aick to drink
iij mornings as is before said.



17
2

Another Laxative for flegme

Take the po of diaturbith ℥j of Diaphenicon ℈ij dissolue these together
in ffenell water hisope water and wormwood water of each ℥ j fl and
give the sick to drinke in the morning warme all procepts kept as before
written.

Pills to purge fflegme

Take Pills of Agarick Arthretice de giug generibus mycobalanor of each
℈j and medle them together with a little syrupe of hisope make thereof
5 pills and giue to the sick in such sort as the pills for Choller as
beforesaid

Comfortatiues after the purging of Phlegme

Giue the patient as much as please you of these conserues following

Conserues of rosemary, flowers of sauge, flowers of Elecampane with
the conserue of roses, of violetts, of prunes of barberies medled together
much an little as your pleasure for these will comfort the heart
and stomack represing flegme and putting away thirst and drought

The preparing of Melancholly and purging the same
A preparatiue

Take syrupe of Enpatory of Epithyma and of fumetary of each ℥vj.
medle them well together with the water of Buglosse of fumetary
of Broome flowers, and of harts tongue each ℥ix giue the sick to
drink warme 15 mornings in the spring of the day as is before shewed

A laxatiue

Take th epoulder of diasene ℥j of the rootes of polipody of the oke
well dried and made to powder ℥ij Diacatholici ℥fl put them into
the water of borage of Langue de biefe and of thyme of each ℥j fl
medle them well together and giue the sick to drink warme an
hower before day and lett him kepe his bed an hower after every
thing kept as is befpre appointed
Then in 15 daies lett him take noe manner of medecine except euery
weeke twice, of triacle ℥ij with a draught of red wine a little warmed
on the fyer first in the morning fasting and let him fas 2 howers
After this sone proceed as followeth





A preparative for Malancholy
Take syrup of thyme of mints of wormwood of Hysopes of Fumetary
of each ℥iiij medle them well with water and parsely rosmarie
tyme balme and hops of each℥ iij and the water of Broome ℥ iiij
give the sick to drinke xx mornings warme in the spring of day
as before
Another Laxative for Melancholly
Take Diarholicon ℥ i8 of the pouder of Sene leaves ℥jʒj of Ginger
Mastiche of each ʒj of the electuary of Succo rosar ℥jʒj medle
them well together in the water of fumetary and of Scabions of
each ℥j and give the sicke to drinke warme an houer before day
all precepts kept as above.
Pills to Purge Maloncholly
Take pills of ffumetary de lapide lazuli and of empatory of each ʒj

If the sicke find not themselfe very well disposed after all this let him
rest a moneth taking no manner of Phisicke.
A soveraigne medicine for the Plague both preservative
and Curative
Take a henne egg new layd and make a hole in the crowne
by the whichi you shall drawe out all the white and leave the
yolke in the shell, the same egg as full as it may bee stuffed wth
good whole English Saffron dry this egg against the fier or
in an overn when the bach is out soe long untill the shell bee utterly
blacke and burnt and the rest sufficiently brittle and dry then
make it in powder putting into it as much powder of mustard seed
as all the whole egg shall weigh then ad therto these things
following Dittany Tormentill nux vomica of each beaten by themselves
to pouder ℥j Putt therto rew pionie rootes zedoaric, Camphire and
fine triacle of each equall porcons soe that the waight of them

in all ℥iij ʒij beate them in a morter by the space of 2 howers untill

A Soveraigne water for the stone
Take Avence Ramsons and the tender codds of beanes stamped
and self sett leekes distilled of every of these by it selfe soe much
as will serve a whole yeare when you will use it to drink take
of each of these waters halfe a pint and as much malvasy
as of all the rest and distill the waters and malvasie as of
together putt thereof into your wine or drink a draught of it when
you feele extreamely your stone and this use once or twice a weeke.

For the straining of an Arme or a Legg.
Take the fatt earth of a mole hill and fry it in a frying pann
till it bee through hott then put it in a linnen bagg and lay it
upon an oaken board and if you see any moystnes then is it
medicinable or els not, that which is moyst lay in the lynnen
bagg to the place where the payne is as hott as you can suffer
it And soe use it evening and morning till you have ease
If you use 2 or 3 baggs at a time hott one after another it
will the sooner bee whole.

The harth and lay it to the navell as hot as you can indure

For the pilling of the face

Take Turpendine of venice and wash it with red rose water till you
haue taken away the smell of the turpentine then take the
marrow of a stagg or for want thereof deere suitt and melt them
altogether but lett them not seeth put into it vergins wax more
then the qantity of the suett and so stirr it still till it be could

For freckles on the face

Take the leaues of white poppy and distill them and with the
water thereof wash your face

For a Timpany

Take flouer delice and seeth them in white wine from a quart
to a pinte and drinke that next your heart ix mornings together

A Fume for the same

Take a Pottle of white wine and a gallon of runing water and
the dung of a ston'd horse and lett it seeth a while then sitt
ouer it on a close stoole alwayes as hott as you can suffer it
of[.....] times in the day by the space of ix dayes half an
hower at a time or an hower if you can

For the same

Take a quantitie of vrine and a quantity of sheepes Tallow
well clarified and boyled together till half the vrine bee wasted Then
take as much black wooll of the sheeps flank as will couer all your stomack
down to your nauell  from the one side of your ribbs to the other, when you
haue taken the vrine from the fyer put the woole into it till it bee through
wett, then wring the Liquor out of the woole and lay it ouer all
aforesaid and euer as the swelling doth descend follow it with the
medicine vntill it come into your leggs Ankles and feete refresse
the woole in the Liquor twice a day at the least and when the
swelling is come downe cleane out of the body lett a Surgeon
make an Issue in each of your insteps and lett it so runne out
till the swelling be cleane dissolued  Then lay a healing plaster
to it and dry it vp, butt in any wise lay not the woole vnto your
legg after the issue made

A restoratiue for a Consumption

Take the cleane yelks of xxx egge new layd lay them in cleane water
halfe an hower and take away the straynes of them and putt to them
a quart of cow milke as it cometh from the cow a pinte of rosewater
the crumes of a penny white loafe beate them all together and put them
into a stillitory distill it all together with a soft fyer, It will bee
neere hand a quarte of water drinke of it oftentimes ix euery day.

For an euill sore in the head

Take neats foote oyle and doues dunge, beate them together till they
come to bee a salue then annoynt the head with it twice a day morning
and evening, when it is almost whole put a little hony to the medicine
and that will make the haire grow when it is Cleane whole within
on weeke or two peraduenture it will breake out againe in little white
pimples, But then annoint it againe with the medicine and it will
bee whole for euer

Against the plague
Take hero grace of marigolds of fetherfew burnet sorrell
 of the croppes and rootes ance a good quantity wash

wash all these herbs cleane in runninge water and bruise them

coole and strayne it through a fayre cloth and soe giue the sick

this must bee giuen before the purples doe appeare the sooner he better if the patient doe brooke it there is now

Take unwrought wax rosin sheeps suett bores greace of every one

A water to keepe a sore faire without dead flesh or proud flese
Take a Alume ℥i burne it and beate it to fine pouder and as

with the same wash your wound or sore but in any wise take
none of the grounds

For could in the stomack and brest

manicule Take a quarte of Clarett wine synamon ginger and

A vomitt

to the patient and it will make him to vomitt the

For all manner of aches of sores wounds or bruises

Take a handfull of Linseede and dry it by the dyer and and beat
it to powder let it seeth in a quart of runing water till it
bee roping like byrdlime then take a handfull of wormwood
seede dry it by the fyer and beat it to poulder and put it to
the linseede and let them seeth together a little while
then take it of the fyer and put thereto a handfull of braue
and a quantitie of bores greace and fry it in a frying pan
till it may make a plaster lay it to the sore and it shal be
whole, if it bee a wound lay a cole leafe betweene the wound
and the plaster.

For the shakeing in a feauer

Take 2 new tyle stones and set them in the fyer till they bee both
redd hott then take them from the fyer and lay them vpon
the hott coales and sprinkle vinegar vpon them till the rednes
of the fyer be quenched then gather of Camomyle, fenell rew
sage of each Mj. and lay the one half of the herbs on the
tyle and the other half on the other tyle and let them
seeth and euer as they dry moysten them with vinegar
till the collour be changed then lay the one half of the
herbs betwixt the hands of the sicke lying on the right
and the other half to the feete tyle and all and they
shalbe whole within three dayes.

For a greene wound

Take Oelondine and house=leeke of each a like quantity

To staunch blood in a wound

Take vervine dry it and make powder thereof and put it in to the wound.

For the same.

Take wild tansey in the tyme of the yeare and preserue it
all the yeare, bruse it and chaufe it betweene your hands
and lay it in the wound.

The manner of purifying sugar
Take the whites of an egg or two and a pint of whey and the water long together till they rise the more they scum the better it is and so you scum out your sugar you must take to every pound the whites of two eggs or one at the least and a pinte of water let them be beat all together upon a soft fire till the one halfe of the water be consumed then lett it runne through a linner cloth and it is well clarified, for to be used in sirrops all other medicines.
To clarifie honey
Take to every pint of honey a pint of water and boyle them well together sryming it well till the one half be consumed, and so shall you have your honey well clarified to be accupied in any medicine.
For the collick
Take a red onion and lay it in the embers till it be through hot and somewhat soft then take out the core and fill it full of may butter or gray sope put it in a lynnen cloth as hot as it may bee suffered let it be layd to the naaell of the patient and lay warme clothes and pillows thereto and so keepe him warm lying upon a bedd.

Sugar roses
Take of tender rose leaves and of fine sugar eadh lbi stamp
them well tegether till they be smale ground and well medled
with the suger then put it into an earthen pott and set it

days bee passed take fine sugar lbi not very smale ground
and meddle it with the other with your hands
more when the xxx dayes be past put it in a boxe and keep
it. It is very good for every fluxe in the wombe and for
evills that come of Choller and tempereth heate and dryness.

Palsie coming or disease coming from abundance of

To take away the heate and shakeing of
an ague.
Take red wine and milk of each alike quanitity distill
them in a limbick and drink it with Ale or beer. It will be
as strong as aqua composita.

For the biting of a dog.
Take Bugle the yolke of an egg and wheate flower temper them
altogether but let the bugle be choped as smale as you can and
stamp 20 hasill nutts in like manner and with a spoonefull
of english honey temper them altogether and make it as
shall have need of it.

For the stitch,
Take set wall and grate it a good quantitie and half
as much powder of ginger put thereto 6 or 7 spooone full
of the water of scabions shake it and the powder well
together drinke it warme and lay you downe to sleeepe
if you can.

For the cough and to helpe a weake stomack.
Boyle the leaves or Rosemary in fair running water
and drink it with your wine.

For the fluxe.
Boyle Rosmary leaves in strong and put it to the
navell in a Linnen bagg

For thirst and unnaturall heate in the

Put of Rosemary water boyled into the wine that they drink

A restorative for one that is in a Consumption
Take an ould Cocke flea him quick and quarter him in 4
pieces cut of his head and kepe the blood of him and brake
his bones and soe put him in the pott and the blood withall
stop the mouth of the pott close with paste and put that pott
into a grater full of water and let them boyle 4 howers
but see that you have a pott or a chafer and the fier full
or warme water to reserve it that seeth not dry when
the cocke is well sodden take the gravie of it which
wilbe like a Jelly when it is could but it is best to
be eaten hott you may take it with other meates and
drinke it in your small drinkes.

For him that is filone broken
Take Elecampane sheeps tallow and wheate branne make
a plaster thereof and lay it to the sore as hot as you may
suffer if trusse him up with a cloth and give him the
powder of an herb which is called dogstarre in his
pottage ale or wine in the morning fasting let this bee
used 9 dayes and hee shall mendz.

A short instruction concerning the nature of
the Juniper tree and the oyle made of
the berrys thereof being cuningly without
any Composition distilled and fined set
fourth by John Fisher dwelling behinde
St Gilden hard by the little bridge in
Hirshell streete  in Xurinberge.
The Juniper tree doth growe and spring up most kindly
in the forrest the ground being dry and leane and wherever
there is a good and well tempered a yer neither can the
could doe any harme to the tree nor to the fruite for
as much as each of them are hott in the third degree the
same nature of heate hath beene found full often in
them and by experience tryed as for example The
Juniper with his branches and fruite being burnt to
ashe and a burning firebrand hid and covered with
the ashes thereof doth keepe the fier quick for a
whole year together the tree is a stirrer up of age
and preserver of long life and a renewer of age.
The Juniper oyle being made of beryes without Addition

(15)
Addition on any composition of other things and soe
stilled purified and separated from all his waterish
moystures and earthlt grossness and dreggs ought to
be prefered before all the oyles of the world and and considering
his naturall workings it may full well serve instead of
naturall balme it entreth and peirceth through the
flesh veins snnowes and arteries and healeth all
wounds and hurts of the body being taken with wine
broth of flesh or with such waters as are appropriate to
every lymme as you shall perceive in the end of this treatise
doth preserve a man yea maketh him whole and rid of
all such diseases as lightly as may happen unto him untill
his end and departure the same oyle is alsoe good for
all crooked lame and dead limbs the which through
disease greese or stoping of the humours are blemished
It is good likewise for the encleanenes of the skyn black
and rotten leggs cankars fistulaes Cramps swellings
and greefes of the hips soe that the place diseased
bee with the said oyle warme annoynted and alsoe take
with wyne broth of flesh or waters serveing for every part
It is good for the synnowe that come from the brayne
and goe through the neck and chyn the feete beinge
warme annoynted therewith And alsoe if it bee taken
with broth of a hen in the quantitie of 3 drops or with
water of lilium consallium called May Lillie Larke
Lillie or Lark flowers and of hysope and with the water of
the blossome Arboris siliae called quick beame tree soe that
these waters bee duly proportioned to the three drops
of the oyle.
Alsoe this oyle is notable good for all poyson and pestilence
soe that the place of the greefe bee annointed with and
also if ƺi of it be taken with waters that serve for the
heart.





It doth help apposthems boyles and blisters of liver lungs
and spleene it southeth and maketh them ripe and
finally consumeth it quite away without any harme or
danger of the diseased soe that the oyle be taken with
wine or with waters for the parte apointed.
This oyle used and taken as aforesaid is good and helthfull
for the English sweating sickness if ℥j or ℥fs of it to bee
taken wine or vinegar.
Furthermore a quarter of ℥j of the said oyle being taken
in warme wine helpeth the bloody flix.
Ston~ 5 or 6 drops of the said oyle bring drunke with warme
wine cloth ease him that cannot aavoid urine.
The oyle is alsoe very good for women gua mimio mensium
profluvio laborant and cannot stopp the inordinate issue
cause them to take 5 or 6 drops in warme wine it stancheth
and stayeth it inieadiatly and they come to their orderly
cours againe.
This oyle is good for him that hath a naughty stomack
by reason of flegme furring it and cleauseing close to it soe
that it cannot digest the meate that is in it and likewise
by reason of the uncleannes of the stomack breaths and
vapours soe aforesend and strike up into the head and
then the same hurt full moystness and ingender and breed
in many men a rhume or distillation the which in some doe
fall from the head into the chest and lunge in others into
the stomach a gaine in same into their legs and feete and
in others into their Armes and hand whereby aftewards
the limbs become starke stiff and numbe.
Whoseoever is troubld with the diseases aforesaid lett him
take of that by  3 or4 drops in warme wine cloth
mornings and evenings and bit be pungent his stomack
with the oyle by he fur side and certaine times together
bring

If one be stifled in his limbs as a foresaid in like sorte

by taking could is wasted and banished to growe and
come againe rendreth to it naturall heate and maketh

This oyle alsoe used with wyne or waters acording to the

An oyntment for a bruise
Take wormwood Rew Betony smalage of each mj bruise them
together with a little may butter and capons grease and seeth
them on the fier in an earthen pott set at high till it
be greene then straine it through a cloath and use it

For a stroke in the eye
Take the white of and egg with the streme evinge it till
it bee thicke then stampe singreene and put the uiyce to
the white of the egge wett faire towe therein and bynd
it to the eye

To breake a sore
Take a pinte of white wyne and a handfull of sheeps
dunge let it lye in the wyne till it bee broken then wringe
it and straine it and boyle it with wheate flower and
make a pultise thereof and ley it to the sore.

For the same
Take fenugreeke and boyle it in running water till it be
somewhatt soft then beate linseede in a morter with a
little water and the fengreke therewith and a little
barowes greace also and soe make a pultess thereof and
lay it to the sore, it shall either breake it or drive
it away

For a felon or a boyle
Take a saucer full of the iuyce of louewort and as much
of the milke of a red cowe and wheate flower and make
a pultise and lay thereon being warme.

To breake a felon or Posume within or without
Take Bugle, Porrett Calves dung and swynes greace
grind them together and lay them to the sore.

For the same
Take wormewood smallage and swynes greace as much as
of the herbs Bray altogether and doe it in a cloth and
lay & to the felon or a posteme and it shall drawe it out and
heale it without any other things but looke you change
it morneing and evening.

For a bruise or swelling

Take the roots of marsh mallows that grow in
march wash them faire and cut them smaler to the
quantitie of  M my 4 dots and of wild mallow leaves  M my 3 dots
seeth them in a gallon or 2 of water till the third
parte be roasted then take out the rootes and the herbs
and stampe them in a mortar dry, put them to a good
deale of fores greace stamped and a handfull of flax.
seeds stamped as smale as you can before you put it to your
stamped rootes then stampe them altogether and make
thereof a salve Lay it on a lynnen cloth or a piece of lether
of a grate thickness and heate it as hot as the patient
can suffer it before you lay too this plaster you must
wash it with the water a grate while as hot as you
may suffer it that the herbs and rootes were sodden
in and then lay to your plaster

and set it in the sunne the space of a month after
put it into another glasse and take away the residence and soe
dayly chang it untill it be clere and then use it.

Take of Bay leaves the greenest a good handfull bruise them

hours if hee can noe longer endure take warme clothes to
warme him with and put on drye clothes.

An oyle for all kinde of winde in the stomacke

Hand-dwarn manicule

Take a gallon of the ouldest and best malmsie that you can
gett and set it on the fire put to it of wormewood and red
mints of each Mj of rosemary M/8 sage as much of red roses
a handfull or more bruise all them together  and put into the
wyne on the fire put thereto mastich  zj cinamon  zjmaces
zj nutmegs  zjj ginger  z/8 long peper and olibanum of each
zj beate all these to powder put them to the malmsie and herbs
and let it seeth till it be consumed to less than a quarte
then take it from the fire and keele it and strayne it into
a pewter pott and put to it when it is strayned a pinte of
the best oyle olive that may bee gotten and set the pott into
a kettle of water over the fire and seeth the pott soe in the kettle
that there goe noe water therein you must binde the pot
that it fall not nor runne aboute the kettle as it seetheth
and the pott must bee uncovereed when it seetheth that the
steme may avoide out and soe let it seeth till the malnasie
be wasted away then take it from the fire and stop the
pott close and as you feele your stomack troubled annoynte it
with a little of this oyle and you shall finde it a precious
Remedy for all diseases in the stomack

A bath for the palsy
Take of new cow milke of good malvasie and runing water
of each 2 gallons lavander mbj and those greate ones
sauge and rosemary as much of each iiij sheeps heads
the braynes taken out seeth the woole and the flesh

the seething that the steme goe not out and when it hath
sodd a good while sett it by the fire and let it bee covered
close till it bee as could as you may suffer it to sitt and sweate
in it about 6 or 7 times.

For wormes in Children./
Take younge slips of walnut trees scrape away the russet of
it and take the greene that is next a good handfull or

the cowe soe that the strength of the walnut vinde

For the yellow Jaundies./

the drinke then put to it true Ivory and a little
triacle soe luke warme use to drinke of this 4 or 5
mornings fasting and neither eate nor drinke for two
howers after.

For lamenes of Armes or leggs or any other parte
of the body

manicule Take nettles and boyle them in sallett oyle till the strength

A very good aquaviti and the property of it

How the stones of diuers beats and fowles
should bee vsed for restoratiues for weake men
Take the stones of a goate and wash them faire and dry them
in an ouen when the bread is taken out soe that they bee not
burned nor too much dryed but as soone as it is ready to make
powder take it out of the ouen and when you haue neede to take
it, make it in powder and drinke it with what your stomacke
best liketh for weakenesse coming of a flix with red wine
or muskadell is best, In this sorte must you use the stones
of red deere, fallow deere of a yong ramme of the bore pigg of
a yeare moneth ould the hare and the leuerett, these because they
are heauie must bee made into powder & takin with broth or
what liquor you like best or in an egg rosted or potched well
the stones of the goate are stronge in sauour and therefore must
be parboyled as the deere bee. The stones of fowles as of the
Cocke the Phesant the Partridge the sparrow the Larke the
Cockrills

A spetiall restorative for those that bee
in greate weakenes or fallen into Consumption
Take the stones of a goate and wash well the outside of them

likewise sliced the juyce of burrage the inges of savorie
and a quantitie of fine sugar make this in an electuary
with clarified honey put it in boxes and eate of it evenings
and morning and after noone if the weake stomacke
will beare it.

For the migraine in the forehead
Take a nutmeg, grate it, and lay it upon a cloth the bredth
of the forehead wet it with malvasie, and rose water, and
binde it to your temples and forehead all night and if it
ease you not at the first doe soe againe.

after and the fever will away.

To heale any running sore
Take sage leaves woodbine leaves rosemary and hysope of each a
like quantitie and stampe them very smale alsoe take mosse of
the black thorne and burne it and make thereof fine powder
burne alose a good quantitie of Alume and compund all these
togeether with honey and it will bee like a salve ley this to
any running sore and it will heale it.

For an ague
Take sage rosemary vervine hysope of each i. stampe all
these together and drinke the iuyse with ale blood warme
an howr before the fit doth come let the patient then
lay him upon his bedd and cover him well with cloathes
useing this but 4 times hee shall have ease.

To knit sinnewes that bee cutt
Take red wormes either knit or unknit and rid out the blacke
dirte that is in them stampe them smale, and bringe the
synnewes together and let them patient keepe his legg or arme
streight but 24 howers with this layd unto it and the
sinnewes shalbe knitt.

For a stitch
Take stichwort and sea holly of each  i of saunders one
spoonefull boyle all these in a pottle of stabeate (and alsoe
of bay berryes half a spoonefull.) till it come to a quarte
let the patient drinke thereof blood warm, then boyle all the
rest againe with wheate branne and lay it to the place where
the stich is as hot as the patient can suffer it and it will
helpe him incontenently.

For swellings in the body
Take the powder of ramsons and let the patient drinke it
itin white wineblood warme next his stomacke in the morneinge and let
him keepe him fasting 2 howers after and walke upon
it and this will heale hime.





For the swirling of a mans yard
Take a piece of new cotton and heate it in new butter and lay
it to the place as hott as the patient can suffer it this will
help him within 3 or 4 dressings.

Dirt bread

Take liquerice scrape it cleane and bruise it in a morter
and seet it in a gallon of runing water till it come to a
point of lesse, then strayne the water from the liquerice
and put in in a smale vessel and seeth it over the fire againe
till it bee well wasted, then put to it a good quantitie of sugar
and ginger and a few anyseeds and worke it with as much
fine flower as toy thinke meets and make the bread in manner
of roles lay them in a paper in a soft heated oven or after a batch of bread

For the pestilence

Take ren, sage, ellet leaves, bryer leaves of each M. stampe them all together and strayne them through a farie cloth
with a quarte of white wine put thereto a quantitie of good
white ginger beaten to fine powder and drinke thereof a
spoonfull 9 daies together

For paine in the throate

Take cinquefoils Mj. seeth it in runing water from a
quarte to a pinte, then hould the leaves in your mouth for
a quarter of an hower but let nothing goe downe and
you shall have ease

For an inposterre in the head

Take a primerose toots pare it cleane and cleane it in the midst
and make little peeces there of put them in your nose and it
will cause the apposteme to brake





For shrinking of the synewes.

Take lavander cotton mj: with a quantitie of running water
out of strawberrys empernell mj: and 5 or 6 swallows
feathers, stampr them all together and straynor them and
with them with may butter to a salveand for put it in a
pott the vertar therof will remaine 7 years. amognt the
sore threwith then lay upon it aquantitie of woole
an once or twice a day unbind it and worme the woole
wll and lay it to aanaine and soe let it lye all night after

For worms in the stomacke.

Take of wormwood, rew and lavander cotton of each mj:
seeth them in a pottlr of maluasie in a close pott untill 3
pints bee sodden away, then take out the herbs an presse
them betweene two trenchers the put your herbes into a little
bagge, and lay it from the upper parte of the stomacke
beneth the navell.

For ay evill in the boly.

Take centory the 8th quarte of a handfull an seeth it in a
pinte of white wine to the halfe Drinke it every moneth
3 or 4 dayes together and use it./

For the bloody flies/

Take plantanr knob grasse and sheperds purse stampr them
and mingle the with prosset ale and drinke it as hot as
you can.

For the same

Take 2 spoonrfulls of the serde of red roses and make powder
of them with ale blood warme drinke the same and
Continue it 2 or 3 dayes.

For synnewes grieved with could.
Eate ecampion roots with may butter.
For one that is costive
Take mallows or dockes and seeth them well and put in youre
ale and soe use to drinke it.
For the ague
Take of the tops of furrs mjfs and as much of the tops of
rosemary pound it together and strayne it wth halfe a pinte
of sacke and set it to the fire in a covered pot till it bee very
hott, (but let it not seeth) let the patient drinke it fasting
and keepe his chambers 3 or 4 days after.
To make a dry bath to provoke sweats
Take a greate bathing tubbe with bayles on the top of it set
a stoole within the same then must you take three bricks
and lay them in the fire till they bee red hott then sprinle
a little water on them, then lay on ould bourd in the tubbe
and lay the hot bricks on which you may doe with a paire
of tongs and upon the bricks ley Ivie that groweth on the
ground when you are set in the tubbe, cover it with sheets
and blankets as close as you can suffer it and soe sweate by the
space of three quarters of an hower or an hower then let your
bed bee very well warmed and set the tub as neere your bed side
as you can make alloe two or three little baggs of Lynnen filled
wth wheate Grains and sitting in the tub wipe the sweate of
your body with these then enter into your bed and there sweate
a little.
A restoration after the sweating
Take the pith of a apricocks slitt it out of the skynne
then

47
Then take two new Lay'd eggs and break themand laye them in water a while
then beate the yolks of them with the said pith, afterwards
strayne them with malnasie or muskadell and set it on the
fire puting in ʒ dates picked and shred very smale let it boyle
with sugar about 2 or 3 walnus and soe drink it and then
slepe if you can.
For a could windy stomacke
Take a greate handfull of rosemary flowers cleane picked and
sowed in two sheets of paper close that noe ayre or dust can
get to it and soe being thoroughly well dryed steepe them in a
quarte of aquavite all night the next day distill the same in
a still of glasse, as you would doe Cinamon water closeing up
your still with paste that no ayer may come out and with
a very soft fire leasurely distill it and use there of as occasione
shall serve.
To clense the spleene liver and lungs
Take spirage buglosse Borage and balme boyle them in
white wine and a little sugar if you please straine it and
keepe it in glasse, and use to drinke there of in the morning
fasting.
A fume for could and winde in the body
Take flagg rootes and boyle them in white wine and put
them to a handful of rosemary taking the same from the
fire sit over it and rieceave the ayre thereof as hot as you can suffer it.
To take away the redness of the face.
Take the white of a new Layd egge and beate it a little then
take sweete barowes greace and shred it and put it in a
pewter dish over a pott of water boyling on the fire take
the best of it bring molten and fryed and hirms greace used
in like manner also the flower of a cockell seeds which
groweth in the corn and a little ?amphire grind all those things





Things together in a woden dish and take of the same at
night goeing to bed and annoynt your face therewith
and in the morneing following rubb your face with a
scarlet cloth

To abate blood in the face

Take the whitest ginger you can get some call it rotten ginger
make powder thereof searce it through a peece of Lawne or
sarcnett Grind the said ginger with may butter and spreade
it upon a ^faire cloth plasterwise and lay it where you will haue
the collour abated

Oyle of paper good for a fetter or a ringworme
or any other thing riseing in the flesh

Make Cornetts of paper such as grociers vse to put spice in
cut the mouth of the same round and houlding the smale
end of the of the cornet between a paire of tongs or snaffers, with
a candle set fire on the broade end and hould it ouer a
pewter dish and let it burn neere the dish and it will
yeild an oyle with the which you may vse to annioynt the place

A bath

Take bay leaues, mugwort motherwoort, St Johns wort balme
and rosemary of each Mv. boyle them in the lyes of sacke
the space of an hower, then put all into a tub and sit ouer
it in the morneing the space of an hower, and sweate, then
goe to bed the space of 3 howers, and sweate there doe
this for 5 dayes when you thinke good

For paine in the belly.

Take of sallet oyle a pint, of white wine halfe a pinte
rosemary flowere well picked Mj. boyle all together in a
newe earthen pipkin till halfe bee consumed away and
annoynte your belly therewith euening and morneing.

To expell winde and comfort the stomacke

Take a pinte of racked rem[.] wyne, halfe a pinte of runing
water

(48.)

Water rosemary flowers Mj. of sweete fennell seeds brused a
spoonefull boyle all these together till halfe bee consumed put

To aswage the paine in the belly.

Take cellondine Mj. one egge shell and all bay salt Mj. and the
powder of a lome wall powne them well together and lay
them to the soles of both your feete 24 howers.

For wasting in the backe

Take two new layd eggs and breake the tops of them and power
out the yelkes and whites, then put the whites into the shells
againe and fill the shells with the same whites and roewater
either white or red and sugar and set it in the embers and stirre
it still and when it is a little warme supe them vp and lye
a little while in the morneing after you haue receaued them
in your bed.

Another for the same

Take the whites of 2 newe layd eggs and white amber
fine beaten and serced through a peece of sarsenet and
beate the whites a little then put the amber into them
and soe supe them vp.

For the palsie

Take rosemary batony sage maiorane of each mj English
honey halfe a pinte seeth all these together in 3 pints of white
wine to a pinte and a halfe and drinke thereof 9 morneings
fasting.

For the faleing sicknes

Take the rootes of piony cut them smalle and were them about
your necke and wrists like brasletts and drinke the filing
of a dead mans skull





For the shakeing that is a spice of the  falring
sicknes.

Take 4 spoonefulls of whiteroine and as much strong ale triacle
3/8. long peper a pennyworth foie berryes 20. of unicornes
horne 2 graines put all together and stirre it about heabe it
and give the patient.

For a red saucie face.

Take carons greace roell clarified and of camphire 3 the mothere
of brimstone and powder of Cinamon, boyle all these together and
annoynt your face every night goeing to bed and wipe it of in the
morning with a faire cloth.

For the plurisie

Take brokelime and chop it somwhat smale and fry it a little
with sheeps sewett and as hot as you can suffer it lay it to the
place, where the stitch is till you fine ease.

For the pinne and web in the eye.

Take the inyce of celonrie and put a drop thereof in your eye
and it will help you.

For the opilations of the spleene and liver

Take harts tonge horhound endive of each M1 saxifrage
and germander of each ounce fennell rootes parsley roots, smalage
roots of each ounce. any seeds, fennell seeds, parsley seeds ginger
galingale and Cinamon bruised of each ounce capers ounce seedhall
these in a pottle of running water and a pinte of sacke let them
boyle till the liquor bee halfe wasted; then strayne it and
put to it sugar fluid ounce or seven spoonefull of vinegar and
a sticke of cinamon broken in peeces, seeth all these together
with an easie fire a quarter of an hower then straine it
and drinke of it evening qnd morninge a spoonefull at
a time warmed.


An oyntment for the same.

Take diathea ounce capon or hems grace ounce oyle of camomile
oyle of dill of each ounce oyle of sweete alons ounce mixe all these

evening and morning and lay thereon a lynnen cloth warmed

To stay the roockeing of a purgation that roocketh
too extreame.

Take crumes of a fine manchet and lay them in soke in red rose
water; take two or three spoonefull of it and eate it without
warmeing and more if you see cause.

Another for the same

Take a quarte of ould red wine and make it scalding hott with sterle
gadds put them to cumine seeds bruised soe much as will make
it as thicke as a plaster and being sodden in the wine streine
it and put it in a lynnen bag of a hand bredth square and
hot lay it to your navill till it be stopped.

For the flixe

Roste an egg hard and put the powder of mastich into the
egg and soe eate it

To stanch blood when a great veine is either broken or Cutt[manicule] Take salt beefe, the skynne and fatt being taken away and
Lay it on the fire or embers till it be broyled hott then make
it cleane and put it into the wound and binde it fast and
it wilbe quickly stanched./
Another to stanch blood./
[manicule] Take woole that cometh from cloth while it is a fulling, and
make powder therof in a brazen morter, mingle thereof
with the white of an egge, and boulted flower and temper
them together with wine or water and ley it on a lynnen
cloth and put it to the wounde./
For pricking of a thorne or needle./
[manicule] Take faire boulted flower of wheate and temper it with wine
and boyle it together till it bee thicke and make a plaster and
ley it ouer the sore as hott as the sicke may suffer it and that
shall cease the ach open the hole and heale it againe./

may take dill and beate it smale you must lay this to as
soone as you can after the hurte./.

For a canker either in the mouth throate and or nose.

Take scarlett engrained burne it on a tile stone made redd hott
in the fire till it be cole blacke then burne a lume likewise
till it be white and dry then take of each of them a little like quantity
and make them into fine powder and if the canker be in the
throate or nose blowe some of the powder with a quill 2
or 3 hower before meale in the morning, and when they shall
eate wash it away with the water that is for a canker
and if it be in the throate and cannot be washed winde
a cloth aboute a sticke wett in the same water and wash
the throate with it and in all other places where the
canker is. wash it alwaies before he eate.

Oyle of excester for aches bruises palsies and gowtt

Take of the flowers of cowslips gathered in Aprill or may a
pound, steepe them in as much of oyle olive as they may bee
easilie layd in gather likewise in June calamint herbe
John, sage Agrimony wormewood, sotherwood pennyroiall
lavander pellitory of spaine leaves of lorell, flowers of lillies
of each m1. Grinde them in a morter as it were sawce then
wring the flowers out of the oyle and put them into the water
with the other herbs and grinde them; then steepe them
in as much of white wine as will cover them one day and
a night, then boyle the herbs wine and oyle together for
longe over a soft fire till the wine and the water of the
herbs be wasted away, and thus sahll you knowe whether it
be well boyled or not, Take a spoonefull of liquour that is
in the panne bottome and if you see noe water in the spoone
then it is boyled enough, then take it from the fire and
straine it through a stronge lynnen cloth betwixt two staves
Put it into a vessell of Tynne or glasse for noe other vessell
will hould it, you must in the summer annoynt the patient
in the sunne and in the winter against the fire, if the panie
bee greate after the oyntment the sore should bee layd over
with black woole that uer was washed.

T balme water  omfort the stomache.

Take.



Take a gallon of neive ale in a stone pott and put there to of balme
ff1. well washed and dryed againe upon a cloth and broken betwene
your hands put there to of liquerice cleane scraped and bruised oz
and as much anyseede stirre all these things together and soe
lay them in steepe by the space of two dayes and once in 12
howers it must bee stirred; The two dayes expired distill it
the first pinte wilbe stronge the next will not bee soe goode.

Cynamon water very good for a could stomach.

Take cynamon ff1 rose water and white wine of each a pinte and
a halfe breake your cynamon in smale peeces and put them
into a close vessell with the wine and water and soe let them
stand 2 dayes then distill them in a still of glasse and keepe
the first water by it selfe for that is strongest

For the plague or any pestilentiall disease

Take herb grace sotherwort, mugwort groweing in the
fields wormewood of each M1 of juniper berryes which
bee blacke m1 or m1 cut the herbs smale and put them in
a flaunders pipkin put there to of white vinegar a gallon
let them soke a weeke if the herbs bee dry, els but 24 howers
Then take a lemon comon still and put out a quarte of the same
vinegar into the still upon the herbs and soe still it with
a soft fire till you have stilled all in the pipkin for a
grate fire will waste away the stuffe after this put it in
the pipkin againe and put as many new fresh herbs with
soe many berryes as you had before into the pipkin againe
and soe let them soke againe another weeke if they be
dry as you did before and distill them in the aforesaid
manner and soe the third time with new herbs and
berryes as before and at this last time put into the
pipkin of m1 thridate the best you can get oz m1 amongst
the herbs or els 5 orb (oz) of good treacle; Then all
being stilled as before is said put the same in a double
glasse and stop it fast with  e of lethe  found





Last aboute it. And let the partie infected take thereof ℥iiij
at one time and if neede bee you you may give soe much for 2 or 3 days
as you see cause and to a woman but ℥iij to Children according
to their abilitie and soe to sweate 3 or 4 hours or longer if
they may endure, And lett one stand by alwaise to wipe
their face, left by putting out their hands they take could.

For any ache or paine in the ioynts
Take a staggs horne and sawe it in 5 smale peeces as you can
and as thyme seeth the peeces and the dust of the saweing
as much as you can hould in both your hands in a quarte of malvasie
untill it bee halfe consumed then straine is and keepe it in a faire
vessell and as you will use it take of it and warme it in a sawcer on
the fire, and against the fire annoynt the place greeved and chaufe
it with your hands till it sinke into the flesh and lay a faire cloth
warme over it and it will helpe the panie.

For the palsie
Take slices of hot breade and lay unto the place where the palsie
is, soe hott as you may suffer it slice after slice.

A drinke for the same
Take rosemary sauve Betony Maiorane of each mj and halfe
a pinte of English honey, boyle all these together in 3 pints of
white wine, till half be wasted and soe drinke it 9 mornings
lasting blood warme, and by gods grace it will hepe you.

For running of the reynes
Take a new layd egge breake one end and pouer out the white
then put into the egge as much fine powder of mastich as will
almost fill it up, set it on the hott embers but let them not bee
too hott that the egge may roste leasurily with the powder and
alswayes stirre it till it bee a little niore then through hott
for when it is halfe rosted it is enough sup of this in the morning,
and Continue it till you have spent of the powder ℥j

To make sweete water
Take powder of Iris powder of Calamus, aromaticus powder

of cyperus rootes with powder of Cloves and dryed rose leaves, and mixe

A distilled water for some burneing or blasting of
the face
Take six new layd eggs and let them lye in sharpe wine vinegar
a night and a day till the shells bee of them then take white and
the skins from the yeles and put them into a pottle of new milk
with these herbs following, of beane flowers a grate handfull
of daysies rootes and all, of wilde tansey, of woodbine leaves flower
and all, plantane leaves pine leaves of each mj lymons 6 all
these distill together.

For the toothache
Take Crowefoote rootes, bay stalt, rosemary leaves of each a like
quantitie put them in a newe lymon cloth that was never washed
bind them up in little pelletts.

A powder for those that bee troubled with the
heaviness of malanchollike humours
Take of buglosse flowers, of betony flowers, rosemary flowers
borage flowers of each mj Chicorie flowers Endive flowers
of each mjs put all these flowers in a pinte of malovasie
3 spoonsfull of a guacomposta and soe lett them steepe 3 or 4
dayes then straine them out and give the patient before dinner
one spoonefull and likewise after dinner and soe likewise
before and after supper.

To keepe one soluble
Take whay as it comeeth from the cheese and put in it honey
and salt and drinke it in the morneing, and eate figgs that
have layne all night in water and honey sodden together
and boyle polipody in your broth with thyme for to purge
melancholly.

For the same
Make pottage of mallows mercurie, violet leaves any seede
sweete prunes and a little sugar.

For the same









Take fresh greace of a hog and some running water temper these
Two on the fire and warm wash the patients head and wrap
a warme cloth about the head and it will thereby make him
laxative

To purge the head of humours

Take a halfe penny worth of cloves and maces burne them
upon a tilestove let the fime thereof goe thorugh a towell
into your mouth

For the biting of a mad dog

Take the iuyce of worms with white wine or ale and for
drinke it 3 dayes and with the same iuyce wash the sore
Also red nettles and salt will help it

For all manner of aching of wound ot stroke or any other hurt

Take the roots of holyoke and seeth it well till it be tender
ginde it in a morter, and put thereto a quantitie of wheate
flour mingle them together and fry them with oil olive
and as hott as you may suffer it fry it so she sore and it will
ease aching

To purge watery humors in a dropsie

Take of Eller flowers fully ripe, clensed from their stalkes
and of fine sugar well sersed of each ℥ij of Cynamon ℈iiij
of browne mints dryed in the shadowe ℥j Grind all these
in a stone morter vntill you haue well incorporated them
together Then put it in a glasse stop it and sett it in the
hott sunne a fortnight closely stoping the glasse vse hereof
as much as 2 nutmegs at once to keepe you soluble
These may bee alsoe deuised a peculiar syrrupe of roses
solutiue for this for this purpose.

For an inposteme in the body

Take Centory rosemary wormewood, horehound and with white
wine boyle it to a sirrupe, Let the sicke take thereof and it shall
cause the a postime to goe downe, and when it is broken Let
him vse to drinke of the same sirrupe warme.

For an a posteme or stich in any parte of the body

Take the roote of holyoke washed cleane and seeth it till it bee
tender then put out the water into a vessell, put thereto
Linseede and fengreeke of each a like, soe that of them two
you haue as much as of the rootes that is sodden, boyle them
in the same water till it rope as it were birdlyme, Then

The makeing of Rosa solis./
Take a pottle of the best aqua composita put it into a gallon glass
with a pottle of the herb Rosa solis cleane picked and set in the
sunne 3 or 4 dayes bring closely stoped. then take ginger bruise -
Cynamon bruised of each zi whole mace zij any seeds 2 spoonsfull
bruised 2 stickes of liquerice scraped and bruised white sugar Candy
loj. dates cut in smale peeces OO put these into anther gallon glasse
and put thereto you aquacomposita stremed from the rose solis -
and let it stand tillyou have occasion to use it./

To make the same another way.

called rosa solis a gallonput thereto of ginger nutmegs whole -

bruised of the waightof zb, bruise all these together; Take ~
moreover red Corans ziij dates picked and fine minced zii raysins
of the sunne three ounces figgs of algar six or eight muske ~
g. vj. or viij. ambergreece g. vj. of rohite and red Corrall zj. smale
pearles zj. smale shivers of diamonds and rubirs with a quantity
of rohite amber all these being infused in the liquor aforesaid
must bee put in the sunne for the space of 40 dayes at the
least and for closely covered that noe ayre may come into it./

Doctor Stephens aquacomposita
Take a gallon of good gasgon some, ginger, galnigale Cynamon -
nutmegs graines cloves any seeds fennell seeds Caraway seeds
of each zj: then take sage and mints red roses thyme and -
pellitory of the wall rosemary wild thyme Camomill and the
flowers of smals lavender of every one of them iiij Bray -
the spices smale and bruise the herbs and put them in the rone
Let to stand 12 hours stirring it  druers times, Then distill -
by lym becke and keepe the first water for that is best, and
the second by it selfe, for it is not soe good as the first, all the
summer

Summer set it in the sunne./

The vertues of this water
It Comforteth the spirritts vitall and helpeth inward diseases -
that come of could and the shakeing of the palsis and cureth -
the contraction of the srimeroes, It helpeth the cpnception
of women that are barren, killeth the worms in the body -
Cureth the murre helpeth the toothache comforteth the stomach -
Cureth the ould dropsis, helpeth the stone in the bladder and -
in the rames of the backe it is good for a satinking breath
so whosoever useth this water now and then , and not too often
it preserveth him in good likeing and shall make him seeme
young very long./

Flos ungentor
Take rosin parofin of each lb/8 virgin wax and frankensence
of each ziij mastick zj harts tallonwe ziij Cumfre zij melt
that is to be molten an put that is to bee be......... to.... der





And serse it very fine and boyle them all together Then

wine then boyle the wine with all the other ingredients
together and then let it cook till it be blood warme Put
thereto of turpentine 2 drams evermore stirring it till it be through
could, beware your stuff be noe hotter than blood when you put
in your turpentine and camphire for if it be it spoyleth
all your stuffe when it is could make it up in rolles and use
it as you neede for the list plaster that can be made.

An oyntment for aches swellings and lameness
Take thyme Lavander, Cotton, runnings of strawberryes of each
a handful: cut them smale and beate them in a morter then take
4 or 5 yong swallows out of the nest and put them quicke
into the morter with the herbs and soe beate them together
untill you see never a fether whole, then take fresh butter
made in may clarified cleane, the quantitie of a quarte and
soe mingle it in the mortar with the herbs put it in a cleane
vessell and seeth it within 24 hours after according as the
other oyntments is sodden, and streined, keepe it in a faire
vessell and annoynt the patient therewith very warme and
Lapp him very warme afterward.

take of sewet new taken from the sheepe and could a pound -

For a for brest.
Take lauander horse leeke ground fell mary gods of each m I.
newe layd eggs 2. as much barrons greace as a mans thombe
Beate them together ury frir and sm ale put there to as much

For a wound or cutt

A good water for a wound or ould sore

A water for an ould sore

of tanorose and seeth it, when the bagg sinketh it is enough

A salue to drane and heale
Take vergin wax (which haleth cod) which draneth and white

lett it coole

Take a penny pott of maluasie and as much vinegar and a good

it, it shall driue away the ague or alter the dayes thereof./

To knowe whether hee that hath the menison
shall live or die./
Take a penny waight of yard cressen seeds and giue him to eate

For the webbe growing in the eye./
Take the iuyce of gould wort the iuyce of an onyon and as much
honey as of the iuyce of it heate them together and put it in the
eye it will helpe both man and beast./
For the pearle or webbe in the eye./
Roste eggs hard that nothing runne out then cleane them in
the middle and take out the yelkes put therein the powder
of alume glasse then lay them together againe and put them
to the fire till the alume be molten in them put the eggs
with the shells and all in a faire cloth, and bruise it togethere
well in your hand and wringe it through the cloth then take
a faire whet stone and rub white ginger thereon alwaies
wetting the ginger therein and rub it soe that it may runne
of the stone into the water then drop a little drope in his
eye that is sore lett him lye soe and rest therewith and sleepe
doe soe euery morneing thrice till it bee whole, for it will
breake the pearle or will prime and make him see well.//.
For the webbe./

the marrowe of a goose winge and drop it in the eye./

For stinking of a sore

For the gowte
Take a hedghog quicke and sticke him like a pigg and lett him
bleede as much as hee will then flea him, saueing as much of the
greece oppon the flesh as you can likewise draweing out of his
saue as much of his greace as you can take from & him, wash him
as cleane as you can, and all the fatt of him, then stuffe his belly
with a handfull of rosemary with the greace you tooke from his
gutts and sewe up his belly againe put him into a stillitory with
a pottle of the best ale you can gett and as the water stillethe
away from him put it into the stillitory againe twice or thrice
till you  perosaue the body to be enough and not rawe, Then
distill him till hee bee through dry and saue all the water in
a glase & keepeing it in the ayre as you doe rosewater, when the
disease cometh oppon you take morneing and euening 3 or 4
spoonfull and drinke it milke warme first in the morneing
and last at night and drinke not in the morneing 2 or 3
howers after your medicine if you can for feare you must forbear
eggs oysters and wine if you will be well.

For a salt phlegme ./ and red face./
Take roche alume ℥ij brimstone ℥iij ʒij make them
into fine powder euery one by them selves and deuide each one
into two partes put the one halfe into a pinte of white
wine vinegar and the other halfe into a pinte of rosewatere

it together, when the patient goeth to bedd bath his face with
white vinegar warme by the space of a quarter of an hower
and so lett it remaine a while and then wash it away with warme water./

For all aches./
Take a fatt dogge and put out all his bowills and lett him hange a
night that the blood may runne from him then put into him a quarte

put to that greace a good quantitie of Camomill bruised

then boyle them all together till it waxe somewhatt
greene, and streine it, it will bee much better./

A spetiall good medicine for lyms that be are
venomed
Cause to bee made 2 earthen potts in this forme following
the first of 2 gallons full of holes both bottome sides and couer
the other pott must bee soe bigge as you may sett the other pott
within it, this must bee very well beaded within and well neated
soe as it drinke now liquor your first pott must have 3 feete to
to stand vpon of 2 inches longe, then gather in June or July, the
leaues blossomes and seeds of cheekweede bruise them smale all
together and put them into your first pott soe many as it will
hould and in the midst of the herbs, put 3 oxe galls and to euery
gall 2 cloues then couer that pott and put it into the other and lett
that alsoe bee close couered as you can, sett it in the ground where
it may stand dry and there let it stand 40 daies then take
it out and you shall haue a very spetiall oyntment with which
annoynt the patients lyms, and they shalbe restored with gods help:/
For the timpany or dropsie./

you by gods grace./

A good aquavite./
Take a pott full of good cleere red wine and put therein powder of
Canell cloues ginger pellitorie negmeg nutmegs, galingale, spicknard
Maces, cubebs graines of paradise long peper and blacke, caroway,
Ciromontane, cumin, fennell seede smallage parsley, sauge mints
rew, Calamint, horshound of each a like quantitie a drame more
or lesse all these distill in a stillitory with a soft fire and looke
that it bee close and well stoped that noe ayre goe out lett
the fire bee of cole, reforme your water out of a limbeoke in a vessell
of glasse this is not much inferior to balme naturall./
Aqua mirabilis et pretiosa./
Take galingale cloues cubebes, ginger cardamom, maces nutmegs
Melilote of each ℥j of the iuyce of celondine ℥viij mingle all these
made in powder with the said iuyce with a pint of aquavite
and 3 pints of white wine put all these together in a stillitory
of glasse and lett it stand soe all night on the morrow distill it
with an easie fire as canbee./
This water is of secrett nature it clenseth the lungs without
any greevance and the same lungs being wounded or perrished
it mightilie helpeth and comforteth, it suffereth not the blood to
putrifie, but the same it multiplieth in greate quantitie that
almost there needeth not any blood letting, it suffereth not the
blood to bee brent nor yett melancholly or flegme to abounde
or to have domination aboue nature this water expelleth winde
mightilie and profitteth the stomacke marvelouslie it prefereth
youth longe in his estate and engendereth a good collour in the
visage, it preserveth memory, it destroyeth the palsie being
giuen to one ready to sie one spoonefull releiveth him againe
of all water artificiall there is noe better In summer vse
once a weeke fasting the quantitie of a spoone full and in
winter 2 spoone full.
An excellent medicine for an ache or could humor
Take malvasie that is very good a pint  and as much good

To heale any wound or cut./

Take white turpentine 2 spoonefull and of faire runing water
a spoonefull beate them together till they bee as white as a lynnen
cloth, then beat the yelkes of a new layd egg puting to it oyle
of roses, then put all these together, and beate them. If it be
a wound then dip fine lint into it and lay it vpon the sore
if it bee a pricke or thrust you must dip the bent therein and
put it in, this will heale within the space of 24 howers it
not last good no aboue 14 dayes./.

An excellent purgation, for one that is troubled
with a timpany./

Take rhubarbe ℥ss diacridium ℥ij cynamonʒij. a rasine of
ginger and a little sugar beate all these by themselves very
fine and afterwards put them all together, when the signe
serveth to purge, take of the powder one spoone full, in 4
spoonefull of white wine./

Sirrupe of Centory./
Take of topps of Centory ℥4 lett them lye and drie 2 or 3 daies
then steepe it 4 daies in good maluasie a pottle. Streine and
put to it sugar lb i and lett it boyle till it come to a good sirrupe./

For ache in the bones./
Take horehound red fennell fumetary broklime, chickenmeat
and a lttle horse doung stampe it together, with sheeps tallowe
and afterwards seeth it in your owne urine./

For ache comeing of stripes./
Take the rootes of hollokes, seeth them till they bee tender
then grinde them in a mortar and put thereto a quantitie
of wheate flower and oyle olive, boyle them together and soe
hott as the patient may suffer it ley this plaster to the
sore and it will cease the paine./

For the Spleene and other diseases of the sides
Spreade a plaster of mel… on red lether, and applie the same
to the Sore./

A Cericloth for a bruise./
Take swynes greace faire melted lb iii and when it is hott
put thereto of cleane rosin ℥ix and when it is melted put
thereto yallow waxe ℥ix smale broken, when it is molten
sett the pott from the fire and put thereto of turpentine
℥iii and stirr it well till it bee almost could then take 12
whites of eggs well swingled alwayes stirring whilest by
little and little thou puttest thereon the glove till it bee
could when this shalbe used take a faire flaxen cloth and
in this oyntment melted wett the cloth and all hott in the
winter lay it to the bruise and in summer could till it bee
whole./

For the plague./
Take sage of virtue, herbe grace elle cleaves and red bramble
leaves of each M i stampe them all together and straine
them through a faire cloth with a quarte of white wine
put thereto a quantitie of fine ginger in powder and mingle
them together use this medicine 9 dayes together a spoonefull
every day, after the first spoonefull you shalbe sure for
24 dayes and after the 9th spoonefull you shalbe whole
for one year./

And if it happen that any bee stricken with the plague
ere hee drinke this medicine then take the water of scabious
and a good spoonefull of betony water with fine triacle
oz ii mingle them together and give it the patient to drinke
and it shall drive out the venome./

If the sore doe appere, then take bramble leaves and mustard
seeds make a plaster thereof and ley it to the sore. It will
drawe out all the venome by the grace of god. /

For the headach a spetiall medicine./
Tke new lether of sheeps skynne and cut it in proportion
to cover your browes all over unto the temples with two strings
to tye about your head, then with a sharpe knife scortch
it upon the flesh side very much but cut it not through, then
beate beate th white of an egge in a sawcer with a spoone







very well, and smere the lether ouer with the same and
strewe thereupon all ouer sanguinis draconis beaten to fine
powder and soe ley it to your forehead and the temples
of your head and tye it on, this shall driue away the
paine of the migrime or any ach in the head without
fayle.

For the faleing euill
Take the mawes of 3 greate froggs and dry them either
in the embers or in an oven and being beaten to powder
lett the patient drinke it al 3 times in white wine.

To staunch blood in a wound
Take spanish lether and shave the flesh side thereof with
a sharpe knife put a good quantitie of the shaueings into the
wounde and it will presently leaue bleeding.

For the same
Take out of an inkhorne the inke and the woole or sarcnet
in the same and put it in the wounde and it will presently
leauve bleeding.

For a dropsie of water
Take gentian rootes pepper mace of each ℥j mingle them
altogether and beate them fine take euery morning
and euening as much as you can take betweene your
finger and thombe and drinke it in a little draught of
in the morning fast an hower after it and at night take it
when you goe to bedd, and forbear drincke as much as you
can alwayes and eate smale suppers eat alsoe often smallage
pottage next your heart

 For the collicke and windines in the stomacke or in any parte of the body
Take a thicke toste of wheaten leuen bred and prick it full of
cloues and toste it on the wrong side and when it is through
tosted and hott sprinke it with maluasie, and put a single
lynnen cloth ouer it and lay that side to your stomacke that
hath the cloues pricked in it, and as hott as you can endure it
and when it is could heate it againe or else hive an other hott
to tey to your stomacke and it will driue it away in what place
soeuer the collicke or winde and er as the paine fleeth followe
it .ith the toste till if bee cleane gone.

For paine in the sides or stomacke with winde
or for the collicke

Take cumin seedes anyseeds fennellseeds Coriander seeds
parsely seeds and allisander seeds of each a like quantitie
woormewood pellitorie of the wall, unsell thime, winter
sauorie, spike and hysope of each Mjs and shred fine put
them all in a pottle of ale or beeare and boyle them with the
seeds aforesiad vntill it come to a quarte, and put to it a dish
of butter and when it is soe could that you may abide it vpon
the backe of your hand put it into a neats bladder, and soe
lay it somewhatt aboute the place where the paine is and
warme itt as often as it cooleth for the space of 3 or 4 howrs.

For burneing and scalding of hott waters or
any other thing

Take 5 or 6 spoone full of sallett oyle and as much runing
water beate them together in a dish untill the oyle and the
water bee tempered together then annoynt the burneing
them with and it will driue away the heate.

For the wild fire.
Take wine dreggs or vinegar and a raw henne egg temper
them well together and doe it on the sore.

For one that is burnt with a harlott
Take ould flaxen cloth that is cleane washed purne it and
make powder thereof then take oyle of eggs and annoynt
the fore holes therewith and fill them full of powder
and it will heale them in a short space.

A water for the plage or anyother contagious disease
Take rew wormewood agrimonie Celondine, sage balme
marygolde maywede vervine scabious betony feuerfew
burnett sorell rosemary mugwort dragons pimpernell

you still it when you still it wringe out the wine and

stilled of the wine is good for any agues, but put it not
to the water of the herbs

How the stone is engendred in a man
Stones bee engenred in a mans reines and in the bladder after
Cause of ingendring of them are many amonge the which 3
are most espetiall
One is tough flymie matter of which the stone is made the
second imoderate heate, that wasteth away the moysture of
that visious mattere and the remnants hardeneth into a stone
The third is the straightnes of the pores and of the ways and wherby such flymie natter shoud passe.
The sone sometimes is ingendred of hott clammue humours and
Some times the stone is newe and lately ingenderd other whiles
ould and of long contrivuance
The token of the stone coming of hott humours bee these, A sharpe
pricking ach and greeuance in pissing, the vrine is of a high collour
and sometimes hath drops of blood sometimes grauell, and that
is red green or black.
And if the stone come of could vicious humours there is greate
could about that place that the stone lyeth in greeuance
with heavinesse, and the vrine is white swart yellow or of
the collour of ashes.
Sometimes there is grauell in their waters that haue not the
stone as when they are in some hottburneing feuer or haue
gowtes, but that grauell will fall to smalle powder if it bee
rubbed between a mans fingers, whereas grauell of them that
haue not the stone will not doe soe.
Stones alsoe bee ingenred in other places as well as in the raines
and bladder for some haue stones in their stomacke and by
casting they are ridd of them some haue stones in their gut
and anoyde them downeward and in other places, alsoe the
are stones as in them that haue the gowte in or about the
ioynts whether it bee in the ridge leggs or armes.
Signes of the stone be these: The foote on that side that the stone
is, is astonyed, and the ridg bone on that side is greened they haue
paine in pissing and grauell in the vrine.
If the stone bee growing the . waxeth from day to day
full of grauell, and when the .






Lesse then is there lesse gravell in the veine and the veine waxeth
white as milke, and they feele paine as often as they pisse./
The same tokens bee of the stone in the veines and in the bladder –
saur that if it bee in the veines the greevanceis behinde in the
ridge and of that in the bladder the paine is before in the share
and towards the fundament. /
A stone that hath beene longe will not bee healed but by cuttung
a newe stone may bee removed by medicines

The cureing of the stone and first if it bee of a
hott humoour
In the mornring give him draprmuion and in the evening ginger
with oximell and lett him take it with water that harts-
tonge mayden haire parsley seeds and the iiii cold seedes bee-
sodden in. /
Afterwards give him the electuary of the juyce of roses. /
The third day after make him a bath of mercury of worms
savine leaves and ome leaves tormentell Cinquefoile and –
sorrell with burnett. And when hee cometh out of the bath
give him broth that sorrell harts tonge and endive bee –
boyled in. /
The next day after lett him blood in the veine under the ankle
in the out side, if the stone bee in the veines in the inner –
side if the stone bee in the bladder. /
Annoynt the place with Popuelion and vinegar. / And lay this –
plaster theron. / Take sorrell, porcelane and the 4 could seeds –
arach and soudrell and the seedes of fennell and parsley seeds
brate them to powder and temper them with vinegar and white
wine or with vinegar and the juyce of mercury. /
Burne him with a hott Iron in the hamme if the stone bee
in the veines or in the inner side of the thigh ig it bee in the –
bladder.
Take diaprunum and mingle it with the electuary of the juyce
of roses. and lett him every weeke east thereof the quantitie of
a Chesnutt once a weeke when hee goeth to bed. /
Give him a sirrupe of harts tonge mayden hayre of both sorts
politrichion and adiantum Ceter...ch Endive, of the roote of fennell
and of saxifrage boyle all these together in faire water –

And clense it well and put thereto sugar Lett him take –
thereof the quantitie of an egshell full with thrice whereof
soe much warme water and let him use this from 2 dayes –
to three dayes. /
Alsoe take the 4 cold seedes Lettuce seeds parsley seeds of the
seedes of sorrell purcelane and of Saxifrage spikenard galingale
the powder of harts thyme and his blood and goats milke
of all these a like quantitie and burne them to powder make
round balls of that powder with rose water of the white of it
lett him eate one of them at a time and use it from 2 dayes
to 3 dayes. /

The cure of the stone comeing of a could clammy
humoure. /
Lett him use oximel diuriticke or oximel squillitick.
Afterwards make him a bath of fennell rootes parsley rootes
and of march, of caraway and dancus saxifrage and milium –
solid sodden in water lett him sitt in that bath up to the navel
and after this give him triacle with wine that saxifrage and
inibum solis were boyled in. /
On the morrowe open the veine that is under the ancle and
if the stone bee in his veines, let him use this bath of the seeds in the
third or fourth age of the moone and lett him use the sirrupe
following. /
Take the juyce of radish and of saxifrage a like quantitie and –
seeth it over an easie fire. Clense it and put therto sugar or
Take the rootes of parsley of fennell and of merch of dancas
Caraway and of radish of Cinquefoile saxifrage and of bryars
that growe in the feild burnst and langue debief boyle them –
in water put therto suger and make a sirrupe. /
An oyntment for this stone. / Take the huyces of victicella
of saxifrage of radish or burnett or water lillies, and of
Langue debiefe medle these juyces together with oyle of layes
or with some other hott oyle; Boyle them till the juyce bee
wasted away then mingle swines greace therewith and make

And make a plaster of hysope and of saxifrage boyled

together and ley theron or
Take the seeds of fennell of parsley of march of dancus of

tree, stampe them and meddle them with their owne juyce –
make thereof a plaster and lay upon the veines or upon the –

Alsoe burne a quicke hare and grasshoppers or the seede of spurge

Take away the leggs and the heads of grasshoppers or of Cricketts
pound them granum solis and saxifrage to powder and lett –

sodden in and wine mingled together.
Alsoe a foxe eaten is good for the stone and his greace is good
to annoynt his veines withall but mingle it with the juyce gumme
of an Ivie tree if you have it and make thereof an oyntment
powder of goates blood is good alsoe but the goate must –
first be fedd with Ivie leaves 40 dayes and give him white –
wine to drinke, then take his blood and dry it and mingle
honey therewith or powder of aloes and of mirh and of
salt, this powder will breake the stone.
Alsoe the stones that bee in a cockes mawr beaten to powder
and eaten will breake the stone and soe will sorrell. /
And if the stone that is fallen into the bladder, getting into
the yard doe sticke then and lett the passage of the veine –
make a bath of hockes and of betes and lett him sitt therein –
up to the navill and when hee cometh out thereof annoynt
the share before the bladder with butter use this bathing
5 dayes together. /
Afterwards if need bee and not before lett a virgall bee –
put in through his yarde to put downe the stone into his –
bladder where it may 40 yeares without perrill./
And if hee may well suffer it while hee sitteth in the bath
lett him frott from his fundament to his yarde for –
so with often frotting hee may mak the stone to come out
of his yard. /
Another good bathing is to sitt up to the brest in the bathe –
of salt water wherein saxifrage is sodden both leaves and –
seedes and Colewort that have not beene removed since they
were sowen. The water must seeth for longe till halfe bee sodden
away. And in the bath lett him frott softly his yarde –
share downe to the yard and beneth as is before and then –
annoynt him with hott oyntments or oyles to breake the stone. /
The blood of a foxe drinke while it is newe, with white wine –
breaketh the stone and foe doth the juyce of Lymons. /
Many other things are good for the stone, as the stone that
is the spunge of the sea and in the of an ould cocke and –
in the bladder of a swine. /
The stone that a man pisseth pounded and drunke with wine
alsoe the kernells of Cherrystones and of Plume stones bee good –
And the seeds of ash and of gromell and of chicoris Ivie –
harts tonge and mayden haire and the seede of great thistles
with greate white spotted leaves the seede blacke, every of
those hath the virtue to breake the stone, and if they bee –
Compounded together the medicine is of more force.

For the Stone./.
Take romsons vnsett leekes parsley Coriander horshound
saxifrage greene huskes of beanes greene walnutts huskes
greene broone codds hawes stones of white thorne of each
