Another for it and for the Collick alsoe
Take a red onyon and lay it in the embers till it bee through
hott and some what soft, take out the core and fill it full
of May butter or gray sope and in a lynnen cloth as hott as
it may bee suffered Lett it bee layd to the navell of the patient



Lape warme clothes and pillowes thereto and soe keepe him
warme lying vpon a bedd./.

For the Stone./
Take a urnce ramsons and the tender coddes of beanes saxifrage
sampier and selfe sett leekes distill of every of these by
it selfe as much as will serve a whole yeare. when you will
vse it take of each of these waters halfe a pint and as much
malvasie as of all therest distill the waters and malvasie
and water together, and take the water of it and put into
your wine or drinke a draught of it when you feele paine
extreamely of the stone, thus vse to doe once or twice in
a weeke./.

For the same./

you can take vse betweene your finger and thoumbe and
put it into a draught of white wine or racked renish wine
and the wine bring luke warme streine it through a cloth
and drinke it./

Take radish rootes slice them and seeth them in white
wine from a quarte to a pinte drinke heereof everie
two morneing half a pint and vse it a goodwhile./

To breake the stone if it be very greate
Take of a lisander seedes ℥iij beate them smale and mingle
the powder with gould such as painters vse put it in ale
and drinke it evening and morneing 3 or 4 times

For the stone./
Take brode Plantane housleeke feverfew and tansey of the
gardin of each a like quantitie stampe them and streine
them and put vnto them beane flower and 3 spoonefull
of stone honey clarified seeth them ever stirring them till
it bee plaster wise ley it hott vppon a cloth a spoon long
broad and as longe as will come round about your backe
and meet in the bottome of your belly and weare it 2 dayes
or two nights and if it chance to d before that time







Then take it againe a month or 3 weekes after it not once
in 6 weekes for the space of halfe a yeare and after as you
shall see cause

For the same.

Take of distilled waters of hawthorne flowers 4 spoone full
of maluape viij spoone full and of cape ginger in powdere
one spoone full drinke it and it will break the stone.

Another

Take the crops of the rootes of the greate rough mallows
camomill and the rootes of parsley stampe all of these together
and mingle them with white wine streine it and soe drinke
it (being clarified before it bee streined) fasting in the
morning and last at euening.

For the same

Take a quantitie of parsley saxifrage gromell fennell
paristory thyme of each a like quantitie distill all these
together and put 3 or 4 of this water into drinke whatsoeuer
it ale beere or wine and in any wise see that your drinke bee
not new but stale and cleer, It is very good to bee vsed once
or twice a weeke espetially if you neede it

Another

Take saxifrage and watercreses of each mj seethe them
in a pottle of ranie wate to a quarte lett it coole and straine
it faire drinke euery morneing a good draugh thereof and
in the euening alsoe soe that it bee not nigh after supper
it shall breake and auoide the stone if you haue any and
bringe you in ease.

For the stone

Take goates blood foxe bloode and hares blood euen portions
and the berryes of Jvie yew tree filipandula fromwll saxifrage
Colombine seede parsley seede Chreeistones and the bird that is
called a washer make all these in powder and that guie with
water of broome leaues tempered together to him that hath
the stone at mornings fasting and at euenings last and none
other liquour, and hee shalbe whole within 9 dayes and 9 nights.

Another

Take wormewood sotherwood mother wort and hysope
a like quantitie pound them and fry them with neats
oyle long: and as hott as it may bee suffered lay this as a
plaster to the sham and to the backe against the reines vse
this 9 dayes first in themorneing and last in the euening
and then lett him drinke the iujce of tansey

For the same

For the stone and to make one pisse that could
not before in 3 or 4 dayes

Take fine powder of virga anora and putt one spoonefull of it
into a new layd egge soft roste, And lett the patient suppe
it of in the morneing at his breakefast and lett him
not eate for 4 hours after at the least, then shall hee
make vrine in lesse then halfe an hower; if you vse this
continually the space of x or xij dayes as is aforesaid you
shall make vrine and avoyd the stone therein without any
paine or greefe

For the stone
Take the blood of a hare and put it in the sakyn, sett it in
an ear then pott the mouble stopped close with paste into
an hott oven dry it and make powder of it and use thereof
in your drinke.

For the same
Phillipp Combloud
Take saxifrage thyme ramsons of each  iij distill euery
one of these by themselves and put the waters soe stilled into
three sundry glasses Put them afterwards together in a faire
stillitory and distill them againe keepe this water for a
pretious medicine for the stone, and when you feele your
selfe greeued take two spoonefull of the same water luke
warme fasting and likewise in the euenning to bedward soe
that you eate nor drinke lately before, but that your stomack
bee somewhatt emptie and it shall ease you greatly.

For gravell and the stone
Take leaves of radish and distill them in a stillitory as you
doe rose water and afterwards lett it stand close in a glass
three dayes After that for x or xij dayes after euery
morneing 3 howers before you drinke or eate any meate
take thereof 5 or 6 ounces in a drinkeinge glasse with a little
sugar and at night like wise and eate little atsupper.

For the same and for a surfett
Take balme chopped some what smale and infuse it in wine
9 dayes and soe distill it in a limbeck or a stillatory which
you please this water is comfortable for the hart alsoe.

For the stone and to provoe vrine
Take parsley seede beaten boyle it in white wine and straine
it, Afterwards put the powder of amber and sett into it and
soe drinke it warme.

A powder for the stone
Take greene samper and dry it in the sunne and make
powder of it and drinke it with wine beere or ale and drinke
saxifrage water two howers after it.

To breake the stone
Take the clawes of a hogg dry them in an oven and with a
fyle make them into powder Lett the patient drinke each
morneing fasting and last in the evening as much of the
powder as hee can take upon a knives point and drinke it
with white wine.

To provoke urine
Take the shells of snayles faire washed dry them against
the fire in a fair dish or in the ouen and brake them to powder
drincke of the same in white wine as much as you can take up
on a knives point and it shall cause the patient to to make urine.

For the stone
Hill Prat
Take parietary crops of thyme crops of mercury and crops
of parsley of each of them a like quantitie boyle them all
in malvasie and streine it drinke thereof euery morneing
or else take it in your pottage or in possett ale but malvasie is
best.






For the Strangury
When ones water doth stopp in him dor that hee cannott make
a reasonable quantitie at once and that which hee maketh cometh
foorth with marvelous paine of heate and burneing in the yard
by reason of such excoriabious as the same hott water maketh
in the avoyding therof. /

Mr Gilbert
Dr Burne of
St Omres

For help whereof /
Take good sene leaves with the codds or pods of the same z i in the even=
ing ley it to steepe in a pint of good cleere possett ale (looke it
bee warme when you put it therein) and soe lett  thein stand all night
In the next morneing early seeth the same with an hott fire
to the one halfe then 3 or 4 howers before the patient shall
eate any meate, see that it bee cleane strained, temper it with
a little finger and warme it on the fier and soe drinke it all up
and it shall purge you well within a day or 2 after if the payne
doe continue either with heate of your water or paine therof
in stopping thereof then tak 3 pints of Spring water in the
which when it is warme you shall putt in of a seede called Cicer
z ii (it is almost like to a little pease) a little roote of fennell
and 2 rootes of parsley bothe of them picked cleane washed
and the pitch taken out and if you can gett any percipere
take two or three rootes of them alsoe woth the herb and all
cut all these rootes and herbs and putt them into the said three
pints of Liquour and the seedes of Cicer with Liquerice cleane
pared as much as your finger and 30 grate raysins the stones
of them taken out and lett all lye in steepe together from the
evening untill the next morneing, Then seeth them together
on a soft fire to the one halfe straine it and put it into an
earthen pott stopped close drinke therof in in the morneing
warmed on the fire a good draught every time. And if it bee
bittre you may if you please tempre it with a little sugar –

After this soe taken if your water doe stoppe noe longer but
that you mak good store of it though it bee painefull of the
heate and burneing then take noe more of this drinke in any
wise for it shall doe them rather much hurt then any good
It shalbe good to use for this disease to drinke in the morning
forete milke of a goate or of an ewe nwew milked with sugar
somwhat warmed.

For the Strangury
Take of mellons seede and cucumres seedes of each z ii taken
cleane out of the hulls and beaten with blanched Almons and
with a pint of water wherein a good handfull of barly hath
beene sodden before make an almon milke well seasoned
with sugar drinke it warme in the morneing fasting and in
the evening an hower or two before you goe to bed. /

For the same /
Boyle a litle Liquroice in springe fumetary water or conduit
water Coole it and keepe it in a glasse drinke your wine allayed
with it. /

Another
Take the crops or the rootes of grate frough mallowes Camomile
and the rootes of parsley, Stampe these together boyle them
with whitewine clariefie it, straine and drinke it fasting in
the morning and last at evening. /

For the same
Mr Wood
Take alkakengi zi brate it to powder and put it unto inlepr
of violetts zii or els twice soe much whay clarified and some
sugar to sweeten it, take it luke warme in the morneing
fasting and eate not in 3 howers after your alkakengi must
bee kept dry as you keepe sugar els it will relent in moyst
wether. /

For the back weakened with the Strangury/
Take a good handfull of almonds well wiped and brate them
hulls and all then take x or xii dates the stones plucked
out and brate them very fine with the almons. Put to them
muskadell to keepe the almons from oyleing afterwards
put to these more muskadell almost a quarte, keepe it in
a faire glasse and lett the patient every morneing take
3 spoonefulls fasting for a weeke together or more and drinke
not after this for the space of an hower. /

For stopping of the marras
Take nettle leaves grind them and put them within alsoe
nettle seedes drunken with wine looseth the swelling and stoppeth
the maras. /
For the same.
Take lovage hysope wormwood and sallo[.] leaves seeth them
in water and make a plaster from the navell unto the ~
privitie before and behinde, also make her to brooke and
shee shall bee well. /
Another. /
Take reroe and seeth it oyle make heere of a fumigation upon

To make a woman to conceave
First thou shalt knowe that the woman may not conceave ~
for the receit is for large, for to make it straight use this
medicine./
The medicine./
Take powder of alume sandragon and arnemett and medle
these well together with strong vineger and it shall close ~
Another.
Take the roots of greene Centory and grinde it and lay it
to and it shall close - /
To make a woman to conceave
Take hemlock and seeth it in water and give the woman
drink therof in the morning and that same day shee -
shal be able to conceave./ and if shee conceave shee shall -
brooke that drinke soone after /
Another
Take the braine of a crane and medle it with ganders grease

and fox grease and keepe it in a vessell of silver or of gould and at
what time thou wold have knowledge annoynt theresooth thy
yard and shee shall conceave./
Another for the same.
Take niyce of Safrion that is Cockspintle and annoynt thy yard and
the womans privitie alsoe and strane above powder made of the
matrix of a hare and then deale with her, and shee shall conceave/
Another for the same . /
Take the matrix of an hare and roste it and grinde it and give -
a woman to drinke and shee shall conceave./
Another./
Take the herbs Codds and bruise them to powder nianewe
earthen pott and putt in it wine and put therto a gall of a goate -
and give her to drink and the same day shee shall conceave./
To keepe her conceaveing
Take the hare that is with Leveretts and slit the hare alonge an
the backe and take out the Leveretts and the erndds that you -
finde in the wombe of the leveretts and give her to drink and
shee shall not look for the seede./

Take date stones and breake them and give the woman to drinke

To deliver a woman of a dead childe
Take 9 leeke blades and scald them and binde them to her wombe
about her navell, and it shall cast out the dead child and when
shee is delivered take away the leeke blades or shee shall utter
all that is within her wombe
For greevance after her travell

plaster upon the privie partes

For the emerods
Take and drinke milfolis and grinde it and plaster it thereto

Another

Another

Another

For the emerodes that swell and bleede not

Another
Take hares hayre powdered and put thereto and it stauncheth the bleeding thereof
For a sore yarde
Take wheate branne and seeth it in water and draw out the
liquour and put thereto a little oyle debay and heate it in a little
pott and make the sicke to hand his yard in that pott and drawe
it in and out as hott as hee may suffer it and this shall release
his soreness and clense the raiones and helps it and if it
tarry of this healeing put thereto powder of Letarde
or of burnt leade or of black plumbe
For ones yard scalded
Take lynseede or lynnen cloth and burne it and put the
powder thereof in a cloth and binde it bothe sore and it
shall helpe
Fort he faling evill
As soone as the sicke falleth flea a hounde and take
out his heart and his gall and grinde them together
and give him to drinke and hee shall arise and no
more fall





Another for the same./
The first time that you see a man fall take of thine owne
shooe and pisse in it and wash well the shooe and put the pisse
in his mouth and hee shalbe whole./

Another experimen for the same./
Take a thrush and slitt him on the baike and take out his
mawe and winde it in a cole leafe and burne it in a new earthen
pott and make thereof powder and give him to drinke when
hee falleth and if hee bee not whole at the first time make
powder of another thrushe and soe doe till hee bee whole
and doubt not of the cure./

Alsoe swallowes often times eaten delivereth a man of the
evill./

For the eyes./
Take the white of an egge and beate it well and skime it
and put thereof in the eye and it avoydeth all pricking./

Another for the same./
Take wormewood and womans milke and rosewater
and meddle them well together and plaster upon the eyes
and it avoydeth the akeing and the blood and the pricking

Another./
Take new ground wormewood with the white of an egge
and ley it to the eye all night it voydeth the blood and rednes./

For bleared eyes./
Take the Juyce of Cuckooes pintle and annoynt the eye
therewith./

For a pricke in the eye
Take the ashes of snayles brent with her shells and put
thereto and it will helpe within 3 dayes./

For eyes that are sore with swelling
Take betong ground and emplaster it./

To saunch teares./
Take and eate betony and it helpeth./

To cure a pricke in the eye
Take oyle of notts and put it into the eyes for to ripe the
pricke before the sight./

To avoyd the pricke princes and webs in the eye./
Take the marrowe of the winge of a gander and the flower
of barley and heate the marrow first and put a little of the
flower thereto and medle it well together and lay it to the
eye could and it shall brake the webbe and burne it white./

Another for the same./
Take the gall of any fowle that liveth saveing the raven
and put into the eye and it voideth prickes and webbs./

Another.
Take wine that tormentill was sodden in and give it to
drinke to the sicke without any other drink 3 or 4 dayes
and the herb sodden and plastered to the eyes every night
it restoreth theire sight that hath cleere eyes and see not
though there sight were lost three yeares before./

For watery eyes./
Take a leafe of the red cole and annoynt it with the gleare
of an egge and lay thereto anight/.

For deafenes./
Take the juyce of the leaves of wild nepr and put it unto
your eare and it voydeth the akeing and helpeth and
restoreth the heering./

A medicine for the stone

Take ratts doung two parts and one parte of gramill and grinde

Another

Another

Another

Take the blood and lungs, the kidney and stones the yarde

you shall see a marvale, that if the sick had a ring on his
finger with a stone, after that hee had eaten 3 morsells the
stone would start out of the ringe and the man anon goe
pisse but this must bee of a goate of the field.

Another

Another

Take egrimony 2 ounces and peper cornes and plantane and argolth
and verdegrace of each an ounce middle all together and lay
thereto and it clenseth the place.

For burning and scalding

Take the harie of an hare and shred it small upon the scalding
and it shall heale anon.

Another for the same

Take the roote of vergin burne it and middle it with the white
of an egge.

Another

Take oyle of eyryn and waxe of each a like quantitie and middle
them together on the fire and then annoynt thees Alsoe if it be
annoynted before at the first with the yelke of a rawe egge it
shalbe whole anon.

Another

Take lynnin cloth and burne it to powder and medle that powder
with the yelke of an egge and annoynt it a little and lett it
dry in, and alway when you would annoynt lett the ould lye still
and annoynt it a little on the ould, and for use it till it bee whole
and you shall soone see it fall of and a faire skynne waxe
under.

Another for a burne that is newe

Take a mouse skynne new flayed and lay it to the burne and take
it no away till it bee whole.

for any member that is scald or burnt

Take luke warme wine or pisse and put the member soe scald or
burnt into the same and it shall helpe another is to wash
the sore with salt and pisse, the yelkes of eggs is very good.

Another

Take seeded leekes and grinde them and lay thereto and it will helpe.

To dr draine any of iron or wood thorne or arrowe out
of a wounde.

Another

Take ganders lounge and lay to the sore and it helpeth

Another

Take the ashes of swallows and meddle therewith vinegar
and lay thereto and it draineth out the impediment.

Another

Take polipody and grinde it with greace and it helpeth likewise
betony grounded doth the same.

Another

A Soveraigne drinke for woundes

Take the crops of red coleworts the crops of red nettles and the
Crops of red brambles, and the Crops of red fennell of hempe and the
crops of tansey and the roote of madder of each a like quantitie
grinde each by himselfe saucing the madder of it as much as of
the rest and medle all together and make thereof pelletts as
bigg as a nutt and drinke a pellett at morning and another at
evening with what kinde of drinke you list and of this till
hee be whole and lay on the wound a could leafe alway and
nothing els.

Another

Take redd cob seede and tanssey seede and hempe seed of each
a like quantitie and the roote of mader as much as of all the
rest and make pelletts at morning and evening and drinke
thereof untill it bee whole, this may bee made in August for
all the yeare after and in that forme drinke 7 nights before
midsomer for all the year after.

To know whether a man shall live or dye

Take motherwort and lay it under the patients head unknown
unto him if hee sleepe hee shall live or els not.

Another

Take the patients urine and cast it upon a stinging greene
nettle, and if it appeareth greene the next morning hee
shall live, and if it appeareth white or changed hee shall [not]







Another
Take the iuyce of mouse eare and annoynt the poulses and
temples of the sicke and if hee sweate hee shall live els not/

Another
Take daysies and give the sicke to drinke and if hee cast it
hee shall dye if it remaineth wit him hee shall live/.

For all fevers
Take could leaves and seeth them in strong vinegar and grinde them
with oyle of roses and plaster it vpon the stomacke alsoe doe the
same with the leaves of a sally tree and the leaves of morrell
for the tertian feaver give him to drinke plantaine./

Another
Take the iuyce of nettles and give the sicke to drinke 3 dayes
fasting with ale./

Another
Take 3 graines of Coriander and give him to sup with an egg.

For the quartane ague
Take rye meale and the iuyce of vervine and of houseleeke
and of nettles and of plantane and medle them with
4 eggs and make a plaster thereof and cover all the wombe./

Another for the same./
Take swallows dounge and drinke the same and it helpeth.

Another./
Take the iuyce of plantane and drinke the same with luke
warme water and honey 2 howers before the accesse of
the fitt and it voydeth the quartane./

Anothere
Take dreggs of ale and seeth it and scume it well and if
it bee a pinte put thereto 8 pennywaight of the powder
of peper and vipero./

A medicine against all perrills and ach in the head
Take the iuyce of rew and temper well the same iuyce with
good eysell and annoynt te temples and the forehead./

Against the faleing euill./
Take a hound and flea him and as soone as the sicke faleth
take out the heart of the hound and his gall and grind them
together and giue the infected to drinke and forthwith hee
shall rise./.

Against too much flowres of women or the
superfluitie thereof./
Take an hares foote and burne it to powder and lett her drinke
of the powder in stale ale first and last till shee bee whole

Another/.
Take alsoe Confrey and wash it and stampe it and seeth it in white
wine and make a plaster and lay to her nauell another to her wombe
and another to her reines./.

To make a woman to have her flowers that bee destroyed./
Take the roote of gladid and seeth it in white wine and when it is well
sodden sett it on the ground and lett her stride ouer it and couer
her with clothes soe that the ayre may not passe but goe into
her privitie prouided that it bee circumspectly looked vnto
that the woman bee not with child./

Another./
Take colerage and seeth it in white wine and lett her obserue
the order before subscribed as hott as shee may suffer it./

For the dropsie./
Take the iuyce of horselme and Elecampane and temper them
with white wine and drinke thereof 4 dayes and all the euill
will passe through the fundament./

To make a sore to breake./
Take the lillie roote and red plumtree leaues Liuer seede orpin
this is good for any sore aboute a man./

Another for a woman./







Take wheate bread and grate it and put oyle therto and boyle
them on the fire and as hott as thou mayst suffer it lay it to
the sore sundry times. /

A medicine for the toothache. /
Take the powder of a hounds tooth and put to the sore tooth
and it taketh away the ache. /

Another
If thou wash thy mouth with wine that sparge was sodden
in they shall never ake. /

To destroy wormes in the teeth. /
Take henbane seed and port seede and frankensence and put them
in a pott and put in that a pipe sett the end of the pipe to the
tooth that is sore and sett a bason of water under thy mouth
and thou shall see the wormes fall into the water. /

A remedy for hollowe teeth
Take a corne of salt and winde it in a webb tat bee white
and cleane and soe put it in the hole of thy tooth and it shall
heale it. /

A powder to make teeth fall out
Take tithuall and Ellebories the white and the blake henbane
and Pelleter. Take the rootes of all and then take raurns
dirt Galbranum and the braines of a partridge and red corrall
and throstles bodies and lissards or piseners and the roote
of the Siccamer tree of each a like quantitie burne all in
an earthen pott new made and make powder thereof and
with that powder touch what tooth you will with a fether
and it will fall out presently. /

For the palsie
Take mustard and aquevite and annoynt thy armes and thy
wrist against the fire use this 3 dayes and bee whole. /

For the dropsie
Take and make worts of … and watercrisses unsett
porett and mallowes and barly meale when this is made eate
them greene and it helpeth. /

For an Imposteme. /
Take Plantane herb John and mouse eare and seeth them together

desroe and then drinke it first and last as aforesaid. /

Another
Take waleworts rosted and ground and drunke with mede

Another
Take dry figgs and slitt them and fill them with fennell seedes

For the Cough
Take 3 or 4 of figgs 3 or 1 Maruby i horehound lett these two

Against Stammering
Take the pellets made of Castor and pelleter and the roote of the

Take rew ans pelleter sage and Canell and senry and salt and boyle

If thy tonge or throate be swelled stike it with a cleane sauer

Take the roote of piony broken and hang it aboute thy necke

A medicine for the red flixe.
Take saffrone and moyst with liquour and fill a nut shell
with the saffron and lay it above on his navell and binde

and if hee shall live it shall helpe him – /.

Take an handfull of plantane and lett it bee broken and sodden

leecke make milke of almons of this water and then lett the

For Costuirnesse
Take an oz of leaves of Cawrell and 4 oz of leaves of Lovell

For the kinke.
Take greene wort Stoikes and breake them in a cleane mortar

upon his heart /.

For casting. /
Take 2 partes of juyce of fennell and 3 partes of honey and

Take hengle seede and lettice and suger and medle it with

Another to cause a man to sleepe. /

For a cleane face.
Take the roots of the lillie 2 oz of swines greace 1 oz of
powder of ginger 1 oz of powder of cloves 1 oz grinde first the
rootes then medle it well with greace then put therein the powder
and then put thereto Doz of merair and medle it all together
finely uppon a millers stone in the manner of an oyntment
then make the man eate and drinke well by the fire either ale
or wine and make him heate his face well by the fire and this
must bee on nights and then annoynt thy face and goe to bed
and in the morneing wash thy face with water of roses and
use this and hee shall have a cleane face. /

Another. /
Take sage and the milk greace of a swine that touched

 A medicine for a hott ague in the body./ .





Take Spinogre Collumbines turmentill and maifelon and

A medicine for the axes. /
Take rewe wormewood herbe brinnett and white wine
take of each thereof an handfull and stampe it in a mortar
and wringe out the juyce and put there to a pinte of the
aforesaid wine and drinke thereof 2 or 3 dayes. /

For the Collicke. /
Take ginger Canell galingale of each doz. of maiz 2 oz
of cloves and nutmeggs of each 1 oz anyseedes fennell
seedes, Alisander seedes Comyn seede of each 1oz flower of
Canell paristory seede of each doz. and brate all these in a
mortar and make powder thereof, then take seede of rewe
by it selfe and make powder thereof and to a spoonefull
of the other powder put some of the powder of rewe
and put thereto sugar to restraine the bitternesse and
use this powder morneing and evening in wine or in ale.

Another for the same. /
Take a henns egge and make a hole on the toppe and dor
out the yelke and the white and wash the shell cleane
within and take the same yelke and put it againe into
the shell and take powder of longe peper and put thereto
untill it bee thicke of the powder, and stirr it well
together and sett it in the hott coales and lett it stand
till the shell bee all blacke and then take it from the
coales and bruise it in a morter shell and all to powder
and drinke it morneing and evening this is good for a
… and winde in a mans body. /

For a wenne. /
Take 3 prickes of more of berberin and pricke therewith
the vaine till it bleedeth and after that it hath bled wash it
away with hott water and use this every day till it bee
washed away. /

For a Corne -
Take a quantitie of fatt bacon and smallage and sage
of each a like quantitie stampe them all together in a mortar
till they be well ground and then make a plaster and lay to
the Corne and use this every day with a new plaster till it bee
whole. /

For one that is Costive - /.
Take the yelkes of a new rawe egge and as much of white salt
and temper them together and then put it in a cleane cloth and
binde it with a thredde and put it into the towell of the sicke
and hee shalbe delivered.

For wormes in the mawr
Take rew and seeth it with eysell and bruise it and make a plaster
thereof and lay it upon the stomacke of the sicke and that shall
kill them and cast them out. /

For evill hearing. /
Take 3 drops of his owne water and put it in his eare every
morning and it shalbee whole. /

Another
Take plantane and steepe it in wine and drinke it each day

For the tooth ach.

Take the roote of the 5 leaued grasse and seeth it well in wine
or vinegarr and hould it as hott as you may suffer it a good while
in they mouth and it shall expell the akeing.

Another for the same.

Take and shaue harts horne and seeth it will in water and with
that water wash thy teeth and hould it hott in thy mouth a
good while and after that thou shalt neuer haue the toothach
again.

For akeing of an ould tooth

Take rauens dounge and put it in the ould tooth an it shall
breake the tooth and kill the akeing and make the tooth fall out.

Another./
Take the flower that is made of cockle seede and put it in the
hollow toothe and it helpeth.

Another.

Take Ivie berryes and seeth them well in vinegar or wine and
when they bee well sodden drinke the liquour as hott as thou
mayest suffer it and hould it in thy mouth till it bee could
and when it is could cast it out and take newe and doe soe
3 or 4 times and it shall helpe thee.

To fasten teeth.

Take the rootes of vervine and seeth them in ould wine and wash
thy teeth therewith.

To clense teeth and make them white

Take the rootes of mallowes and rubb they teeth and thy
gummes therewith and after that stiff lynnen cloth and
doe the same.

Another

Take barly meale and medle it with honey and with salt
and rubb thy teeth oft therewith and it shall clense them

Another.

Take rye meale and salt and honey be euen portions and medle
them will together and therewith rubb thy teeth 3 or 4 times
in the day and after that wash them will with water and it
shall



shall take away the blacknesse.

Another for rotten and stinking teeth

Take sage and stampe it in a mortar and put thereto
as much salt, and make little plasters and put them
into an ouen vntill they bee baked as though they were
burnt and with that rubbe well thy teeth morning and
euening .

To make teeth to growe .

Take hares braines and seeth them and annoynt the gummes
with them and then take the braines of an henne and
rubbe the gummes of a yonge childe therewith and it shall
make them growe without any sore or akeing.

To destroy wormes in teeth .

Take powder of pellitory of spaine and temper it with
honey and make a little lagge ball and put it in thy tooth and it
shall kill the wormes

To destroy wartes or wretts .

Take equionie and stampe it with gray salt temper
it with vinegar and lay it to the warts.

Another.

Take alia binde with a packe thred in wort leaves and
put it in hott ashes and roste it a while and then take
the herbe and stampe it well and put thereto a good
quantitie of powder of allum and a little vinegar and
mingle them together and keepe these in a box and annoynt
the warts and this destroyeth them as alsoe dead flesh.

Another.

Take soiotsokell stampe it with vinegar and salt make

Another

Take the yolke of an egge and roste it hard and stampe

oyntment and 2 or 3 times in the day and annoynt the wound

For a Corfie
Take henns bones and eggshells and burne them smale and make
powder thereof and cast it upon the dead flesh and this powder
will frett away the dead flesh without any dissase. /

For the tooth ache and heacache. /
Take the seede of Lovage and stampe it and drinke it with
wine and it shall take away the akeing. /

Another
Take nicence and doves dounge and wheate flower of each
an oz. and temper it with the white of an egge and where
the head acketh binde it thereto and it shall doe away the
paine of the head. /

For a sore throate. /
Take wood rose and stampe it and drinke the juyce thereof
with good ale. /

For the voyce. /
Take the juyce of white horhound and water cresses
and         of each a like quantitie and seeth them in
honey and make it in the manner of an electuary and use it. /

A powder for the stomacke. /
Take powder of ginger Canell galingale and mints and
use them with a litle wine or ale in the morneing and in
sawer at evening and it shall profitt thee. /

For boyleing in the stomacke. /
Take the roote of fennell and the roote of ache and stampe
them with white wine and give the sicke to drinke. /

For the stomacke that is engleymed. /
Take the fine leaved grasse and stamp it in a mortar
and take the juyce thereof and drinke some with a little
wine or ale and it shall deliver him both above and
beneath. /

For burneing in the stomacke. /
Take a corne of enoense and swallow it whole and it
putteth away burneing, like wise rawe comyn or any

seeds

anyseeds doth the like being eaten. /

For the bloody flixe. /
Take Plantane and yelkes of eggs and powder of peper
and stampe them in a morter and take the juyce of them
and faire flower of wheate and medle them together and
make a cake and bake it in the ashes and make the sicke to
eate thereof as hott as hee may suffer it and this shall heale. /

To knowe whether hee that hath the flixe shall
live or dye. /
Take a penny waight of Towne cress seed and give the
sicke to eate and after give him to drinke wine or
water and doe thus 3 dayes and if it cease hee shall live
els not. /

To stanch the flixe. /
Take red rose seede tryed ginger fine Canell of each
a like quantitie brate all these in a mortar and make
powder thereof and drinke it in red wine milke warme
and it stopeth. /

For a man that hath noe stomacke to his meate. /
Take centory and seeth it well and when it is well
sodden take and stampe it well and put it againe into
the pott, and seeth it well againe and when it is well
sodden clense it through a cloth and then take partes of
honey boyled and put them together in a boxe and give the
sicke to eate thereof 3 spoonefulls fasting till hee bee whole. /
and that shall doe away the glett from the hearte and
make him to have talent to his meate. /

Another. /
Take centory and seeth it in water and drinke thereof
thrice 3 dayes and it clenseth the stomacke and brest. /

Another
Take centory plantain and peper and seeth them well
together in wine and drinke the same when thou goest
to bedd and it helpeth. /





Another /
Take powder of an herbe called Caraway and eate it in
sawce but the powder of the seede is better then the powder
of the herbe yet both bee very goode./.

To quench thirst./.
Take the roote of louage and stampe it and temper it with
water and drinke there of 3 nightes when thou goest to bedd
and it shall quench thy thirst./.

Another./
Take fennell seeds and stampe it and drinke it with hott
water./.

To make a man sweate
Take dry Cumin and make thereof powder in a mortar and
medle it with oyle oliue and swines greace and fry them
together and when they bee fryed wring them through
a lynnen cloth and keepe it in a boxe and annoynt the
sicke with this oyntment against the fire both the
soles of his feete and hands and lett him goe to bedd
and couer him well and warme and hee shall sweate./.

Another
Take fennell rootes and stampe them and drinke it with
wine fasting and after couer thee well and thou shalt sweate./

For
Take sauge and stampe it will with vinegar and drinke
it and it shall helpe thee./

Another./
Take sage Cumin and peper and seeth them well together
and eate thereof a spoonfull in the morning and as much
at night and it helpeth./.

For biteing of an
Take a little peece of rawe flesh being fresh and rub it well
on the byteing then take a radish roote and wash it well
and cut it smale with a knife and stampe it in a mortar
and after thatt seeth it well in white wine or in vinegar
vntill



vntill it bee tender and make a plaster thereof and binde
it to the byteing and it shall drawe out the venome and
take away the akeing./

For byting of all manner of venomous beasts./.
Take plantane and stampe it and drinke the iuyce and after
that take plantane and Celondine and stampe them well together
and temper it will with stale vrine and make a plastere
and lay it to the byting and it shall take away the akeing
and draw out the venome./.

Another to auoyde venome./.
Take garlic and wilde sage and medle them together anf
drinke thereof./.

Another./.
Take the barke that is on a walnut and pare away the outer
barke and make powder of the inner barke and put it in
water a whole night and the next morneing clense it
and giue the sicke to drinke fasting and hee shall cast
out all venome./.

For horsenesse
Take the iuyce of plantane that was not washed when it
was gathered and good white wine of each a like quantity
and mingle them together and then take raysins and
pared Liquerice as much as thou wilt and stampe these
as smale as thou canst to powder and then medle it in
manner of a sawce with the wine and with the sawce
iuyce of plantane after that clense them and seeth
them vntill a 4th parte bee consumed, then clense
them and keepe them in a faire vessell and giue the
sicke thereof early in the morneing fasting and in the
afternoone if hee sleepe and likewise at night when
hee goeth to bedd, but before hee tacke his medicine at
anytime hee must take 2 yelkes of raw eggs with half
the white and sup them with 2 Cornes of salte and
vse this and it will helpe within a short time/.







Another for the same./.
Take white horehound more Centory and the rootes of
fennell of each a like quantitie and seeth them well
together and put them in good stale ale and vse this
drinke at euening hott and in the morneing could./.

For the scabbs./.
Take the iuyce of the roote of walwort  take 3 partes of the
iuyce and the fourth parte of Barrowes greace and thereof
make an oyntment and annoynt the scabbs therewith in
the sunne./

Another
Take red docke rootes and stampe them and seeth them with
may butter and after that clense them through a ceane
cloth into a cleane basin with water and soe lett it stand till
it bee could and hard and then keepe it in a boxe and annoynt
thy greese against the fire and bee whole./.

Another./
Take white willowe leaues and seeth them well in a
good quantitie of water and put a good quantitie of
salt thereto and henns dounge and make a bath thereof.

Another./.
Take blacke snayles and cut them a little and cast on
them a good quantitie of powder of peper and when
the liquor is runne out of them cast out the snailes
and take the liquour and annoynt the scabbs therewith./

Another for dry scabbs and moyst./
Take white horehound and stampe it and mingle this
with honey and annoynt thee therewith this medicine
healeth both.

For wormes in the hanndes
Take and wash thy handes cleane and then take a pan
with faire could fire and cast ouer the coales a quantitie
with faire could fire and cast of hen bane seede and hould
thy hands a good while ouer the same in the smoke of the
seede



seede as neere as thou mayst suffer it for heate and then take a bason
full of faire hott water and put therein the hands and thou shalt
see wormes creepe out of thy hands into the bason of water./.

Another
Take henbane seede and bruise it a little and then put it in strong
vinegar and lett it lye therein a good while and then wash therewith
the itch wheresoeuer it bee and it killeth the same./.

Another
Take an herbe that is called dormander or Camidios and stampe
it with good vinegar and annoynt the place that itcheth and
it helpeth.

Another./.
Take mustard seede and stampe it well and meddle it with honey
and therewith anoynt the place that itcheth./.

Another
Take the berry of the lesse morell and rub the place that itcheth
with the berry and it shall doe away the itching

Another./.
Take the iuyce of mynts of rewe and annoynt the place
that itcheth and it shall helpe./.

Another
Take wormwood and stampe it and annoynt the place that
itcheth and it shall kill the wormes and doe away the
itching in what place soeuer it bee./.

For a Corne vpon the toe./.
Take plantane and sage fengreene and alum and the gall
of a swine and make a plaster thereof and lay thereto and it
shall take it away./.

To cause a man to sleepe./
Take the head of a batt and put it under the head of the sleeper
and it will cause him to sleepe till it bee taken away./

A medicine for the dropssy in the wombe and
in the feete and for Costinenes and for glett in
the stomacke/.
Take 3d waight of Scamony and 2d waight of rubarb and 6d
waight







waight of Sene and 3 penny waight of powder walter and halfe
a penny waight of flower of Canell and half an oz of sugar of
Cyprus and put them all together and bruise them in a brazen
mortar to powder and lett the sticke use this medicine fasting
for the space of 3 dayes every day a good spoonefull and hee
shalbe whole and shall have a good stomacke and shalbe cleane
of all these sicknesses. /

For the Crampe at the hart called Cardiache. /
Take an ould blacke snayle and betweene the hornes you shall finde
a stone thinner then glasse being of an inch longe and as broade
of a barley strawe take this stone and stampe it to powder
in a newe vessell that was never used before then take the
Juyce of Jermander and lett the sicke drinke these two tempered
together with what liquour you will and hee shalbe whole at the
first drinkeing. /

For one that is bruised.
Take salwyn or fennell leaves and stampe them and seeth them
with vinegar and looke where the greese is most ley this medicine
thereto and it will draw out all the foule bloode. /

For a broken bone. /
Take 4 eggs both the whites and the yelkes and 2 spoonefull
of honey and beate them well together, take bolear manacke
and stampe it smale and put thereto untill it looke redd./
then put thereto faire wheate flower and medle these all
together and put it in a close boxe that noe ayer come thereto
and it will last 40 dayes, and it thou have a bone broken
in any place and lett it bee sett againe and lay this tracture
thereto well lapped with clothes and splints and lett it lye
thereto xiii dayes and it shall fasten the bone againe and
drawe out all the Corruption and after that renew this
medicine at the two dayes end till it bee whole. /

To make hear to growe where there is none
Take bores greace quicke silver herb Ivie, brimstone powder
of broused leaves and juyce olive and make an oyntment of all
and shave the head and annoynt it therewith. /

For sinnewes springing. /
Take smallage lemke and groundswell hemlock
bruswort of each a like quantitie and stempe them and put
thereto sheepes tallowe and boares greace and a little wheate
branne and fry them well together and make a plaster and
lay it thereto. /

A powder to make teeth fall out. /
Take a greene froyshe in May and seeke a Cockes spittle and
therein thy shalt finde a greshoper and take the froyshe and
the grashoper together and put them in a newe earthen pott and
bake them in an oven or on an herth to powder and if thou
wilt assay it strike it with thy finger in a dogs mouth
and his teeth shall fall out. /

For heart burneing
Take pimpernell and drinke it and it shall asswage it or
take 6 or 7 Almonds and eate them or eate 4 raw pease
or xii wheate kernells and it shall away. /

For a stich under the side
Take a pottle of thine ower owne urine and seeth it with
Cumine till it bee half sodden away and therewith wash
thy side and plaster the Cumine thereto and it shall ease
thee of the paine. /

To make a drinke to cause a man to sleepe
till hee bee ript. /
Take 3 spoonefull of the gall of a barrow fromie and for a
woman of a gelt swine and 3 spoonefull of hemlocke the
juyce and 3 spoonefull of henbane and 3 spoonefull of
the wilde nep and 3 spoonefull of Lettice and 3 spoonefull
of popy and 3 spoonefull of eysell and medle them all together
and boyle them a little and doe them in a glasse well
stoped and put therein 3 spoonsfull to a pottle of good
wine and medle it well together till it bee used and lett
him that shalbe cut sitt against a good fire and make him
to drinke thereof untill hee bee a sleepe and then mayst
thou surely carve him and when thou hast donne
thy sure and wouldest have him to awake take vinegar

and





and salt and wash his tamples therewith and his wound
and hee shall awake Imeadiately. /

A medicine for the Could goute and the spaven
Take Lawrell leaves and boares greace, horehound smallage
Lovage; but take of Lawrell leaves as much as of all the
other herbes and a little oyle and stampe all these together
and lett them lye together 2 monethes and then take it up
and medle them together in a panne and wringe them through
a cloth and lett it stand till it bee could and then put it in
boxes and this is good for the could goute and for the spaven
for synnewes and for bruises annoynted therewith.

To preserve a mans body from fellons and sores. /
Take the flowers of rosemary and medle it with clarified
honey and eate the same fasting and it preserveth the body. /

To deliver man or woman of the Collicke. /
Take the flowers of rosemary and seeth the same in white
wine to the halfe or to the third parte clense it and drinke
it could a spoonefull or 2 or 3 at the most and with a
little sugar or clarified honey relented therein, and if you
can sleepe it is the better, if thou canst not sleepe then rest
thee and it helpeth very much. /

To breake a boyle or sore. /
Take lynseede and stampe it well and wett it and stampe
it againe and take holly hockes and stampe it and putt
it all in a panne and putt thereto fresh sheepes tallowe
and make it hott and putt it on a clowte and binde it to the
sore and it shall doe it away. /

Another.
Take a rosted onyon and breake it and crush it and lay
it all night to the boyle as hott as thou mayest suffer it
and if it bee ripe it shall breake it. /

Another. /
Take branne and womans milke and it shall breake it. /

To make a powder that is good for Laxative
for Collicke for the strangnilion and for the stone
Take caraway seede fenell seede spicknard anyseed Comine

Synamon

Cynamon galingale of each ? oz gromell seede ? oz of Liquerice
one oz. a quarte of venecreke seede 1 oz of Allisander seede
1 oz of Anyseede 1 oz of the seede of St Mary thistle a

of the seede of Celondine an oz of the holes of spurge 1 oz of
Jett 1 oz of parsley seede a pound of sene and bete, beate all
these in a mortar and bruise them well and put them in a bagge
this medicine is very good for many more diseases then is before
rehearsed. /

To breake an Aposteme. /
Take an herbe that is called mayden wort it beares a white
flower stampe it and give the sicke to drinke and it shall
breake the imposteme and drive it cleane away. /

To avoyde all corruption aboute the stomacke. /
Take wormewood, fetherfew, Hysope Comyn and vinegar and
make all this in the manner of a plaster and lay it to the
Stomacke. /

For the axes. /
Take tameryn and putt it in a cloth and hould it in thy mouth
and it will gather up the flegme in thy stomacke and then
abstaine thy selfe from meate and drinke 24 hours and bee
whole. /

For the dropsie.
Take the roote of the Flower de luce and scrape it cleane
and stampe it in a mortar and putt thereto a little ale
and temper them both together and wringe out the juyce
and then take 2 spoonefull of honey and labour them together
and doe away the fome and make it as cleere as can bee
and give the sicke to drinke and after that lett the patient
goe to bedd and cover him warme and sett a Chaire by
him with a siege and hee shall avoyde anon the water of
the dropsie. /

A remedy against the goute
Take a pownd of blake sope and the yelkes of 6 eggs and
beate them well both together then take them of and lay it





vpon flaxe some whatt thicke and lay it to the sore that
aketh most, then take the whites of the same eggs and make
a cake thereof with wheate meale and lay it vpon a lynnen
cloth in manner of a plaster and binde it downe well, and lett it
lye thereon 7 or 8 dayes but take it not away, for it will itch very
sore and draw out the matter and put away the ache thereof/.

For the stone./.
Take the rootes of radish and scrape them cleane and cut thereof
45 rounde peeces of the quantitie of a penny or 2d and lay them all
night in honey and the next morneing fasting eate 9 thereof
and the second day 8 and the third day 7 and soe foorth till the 9
dayes bee expired and keepe thee from meate and drinke for 2 howeres
after and they shall breake the stone within 9 dayes after./.

For the emerods
Take blank and stampe them and stewe them in a pott without
water and make a plaster and lay it to the emerodes.

Another.
Take Lytarge of gould and make powder thereof and temper
it with oyle violetts and make a plaster and lay thereto./.

Another.
Take rew and lovage of each a like quantitie and grinde them
together in a mortar and medle thereto honey and fry them
together and make a plaster and lay thereto as hott as thou
mayest suffer it./.

To dry vp the emerods.
Take organum Calamint, Lavander Coliander salt and Coperasse
of each a like quantitie by waight seethe all these in water and
lett the patient take the breath into the fundament and thus
shall dry it vp./.

To stop a womans flowers.
Take horse dounge and faire blank and boyle them in vinegar
and make a plaster thereof and lay to the navell as hott as shee]may suffer it./.

Another./.
Take wheate flower and seeth it in milke and honey make
plaster thereof and lay it to her nauell as hott as may bee./.



Another./.
Take sheeps dounge stampe them and medle them with blank greace
fry them well together and make a plaster thereof and lay to the
wombe and to the raines and it shall helpe./.

For paine of the milt
Take the seede that is within the blank of the ashes and make thereof
powder and drinke it with ale or wine./.

A remedy for pisseing of bloode./.
Take ambrose an handfull and parsley stampe them with goates
milke and giue the sicke to drinke/.

A remedy for one that cannot hould his water./
Take goates clawes and burne them in a new pott all to powder
and eate that powder with thy pottage and it shall helpe thee/

A medicine for neeseing./.
Take sage and stampe it and temper it with eysell and drinke
or swallow downe the same and it helpeth./

To cause a woman to have her flowers./.
Take an herbe called collorage and seeth it well in wine and
when it is sodden sett it downe and lett the sicke stride over it
as hott as shee may suffer it soe that there goe noe ayre out
but vp to her privitie and when shee goeth to bedd lett her
lay thereof to her privite as hott as shee may suffer it that
it may passe into the privitie./.

Pro igne infernali
Take an herbe that is called motherwort stampe it in a mortar
and temper it with water and giue the sicke to drinke and take
the drafte and lay it to the sore and it shall staunch the fire./.

Another
Take redwormes that creepeth on the earth and the roote of
Blyon that is called ernatt and stampe them together and after
lay it on the fire and it shall quench it./.

A remedy for a man that cannot pisse./.
Take a red onyon and make a hole therein and putt in the hole
blacke sope and saffron and roste it vpon the coales well vntill
it bee soft and lay it vpon the nauell./.

For the swynsie in the throate./.
Take mallowes and hott milke as hott as it cometh from the
Cowe and boyle the mallowes therein vntill they are soft and lay
them to the throate./.







To make a purgation.

Take 4 pennywaight of powder of walnut barke and 6 penny-
waight of Caterpins and 4 3 spoonefull of the uiyce of walworte
and temper them with worte .

For a woman that hath the

Take marygold leaves wash them and stampe them and putt therto
a quantitie of honey and 12 peper cornes powdred and temper them
with stale ale and giue the sicke to drink swift and last till
shee bee whole.

A remedy for a nurse that wanteth milke

Take vervin and drinke it with wine or fennel and eate lettice
and butter and drinke fennell seede with wine or take christall
and breake it and make powder thereof and drinke it with milke.

For the morniall

Take greene walnutts with all the huskes and bray them in a
mortar and putt thereto a little blacke pitch and bruise them
well together and after putt thereto a little quicksiluer and
bruise them together and temper them with oyle oliue and fry
them well together and make an oyntment thereof and annoynt
thee therewith.

For the morphewe

Take red docke leaues in summer time, and in winter the rootes
and make sawce and eate it 9 dayes and that sawce shall
drive it away out of the body then take oyle of tartar and wash
the place where the morphew is, and it shall voyde it away within
short time.

Another

Take the flowers of lillies in summer and of rosemary and terfolly
and still them and take spoonefull of the water in the morneing
and therewith annoynt the place where the morfew is and it
shall avoyde it.

Another

Take the roote of a red docke and stampe it with salt and
temper it with vinegar and clense it through a cloth and
annoynt the sore therewith against the fire 3 or 4 nights and
hee shall bee whole.

To keepe a man from being dronken.

Take betany and eate it fasting and it will keepe thee that day
from being dronken

A medicine for a ring worme/

Take the iuyce of chickenmeate and flower of barly meale and
fresh tallowe of a sheepe and fry them together and annoynt
it therewith.

A medicine for any quicke thing in the eare

Take Sengreene of an house and the greace of an eele and putt
it in the eare.

Another ./.

Take the iuyce of wild tansey and power it in the eare.

To restore haire againe.

Take walnutts greene as they growe vpon the tree and bruise
them smale and take the fatt of the heart of a beare or of a
Swine and lay it to the place and it shall restore the haire
agayne.

To cause hayre never to growe

Take the gumme of an Ivie tree and orpiment and pisiuers
evryn and medle them together with stronge eysell and
keepe it all the yeare and wheresoeuer thou annoynt thee
therewith there shall never hayre growe againe.

To make a man to pisse

Take the berryes of an Ivie tree and stampe them and temper
them with running water and giue him to drink.

A medicine for a womans brest that is rankled.

Take lynseede and bray it with the white of an egge and lay
it vpon the brest and the uiyce of ache and ander and rye
meale and lay it vpon her brest and make her to drinke
the uiyce of ache and of altre

Take smithes water seeth it and skume it cleane and lett it
