For changing of the liver the milt and the
jaindis ./.
Take morrell Jubarbe and barly meale and temper it with
vinegar and make thereof a plaster and lay that plaster to
the right side of a man and cause him to bee lett blood on -
the veins of the spleene and give him to drinke in the winter
sugar rosett and in sommer fumiter and scabions ./.
For the Jaunds
Take hard Spanish sope and a little stale ale in a cup and rub the
sope against the cupe bottoms thereof till thine ale bee white and -
then scrape thereto a little Ivory and lett the sicke drinke it
last at night and hee shalle whole ./.
Another for the Same ./.
Take an aple and cutt out the core and putt it full of saffron
in clorted and scraped Ivory and sett it on the fire and rost
it tender and eate that on to bedward at evening ./
For the yellow Jaunds ./.
Take wormewood and seeth it in water and wash right will -
the sicke therein twice or thrice and give him to drinke
scraped Ivory in wine or any of the other medicines aforesaid ./.
For the black jaunds ./.
Take celondine i lb and a quarter of liquerice and put
them in a pott of with wine or runing water a powder ./.
gallon and lett it seeth till halfe bee sodden away and give -
it to the sicke to drinke ./.
To stanch bleeding at the nose ./.
Take parwincle and put in thy mouth and thou shalt
noe more bleede ./.
Another for the same
Take and binde his hea temples with a lift about the head
that the vaines may not have their course and then give
him smallage to drinke and lay under his tongue a -
perwincle leafe and hee shall stanch bleeding ./.
For scalding of the roofe of the mouth in sicknesse





Take a sawcer full of vinegar and a sawcer full of honey
and put thereto a little allume and seeth it over the fire in
a panne and skume it well and take it of the fire and then
take a fether or a sticke with a woole locke and strike
thereof in thy mouth or supp thereof and spitt it out when
thou hast held it in thy mouth a little while as hott as thou
mayst suffer it, and it will helpe thee. /

For a stinkeing breath that cometh out of
the Stomacke. /
Take an handfull of Comyn and brate it to powder in a brazen
mortar and seeth it in good wine from a pottle to a quarte and
lett the sicke drinke thereof first at morneing and last at
evening. / at morneing as hot as hee may suffer it and it
shall heale him within 15 dayes and if thou hast for great
store that thou mayst drinke a pinte in a day drinke
it and bee whole. /

For the tooth ach and head ache. /
Take an egge and roste it hard then peele ut and cut it in
4 partes and putt it full of salte then take a thred and binde
it fast together and blisse it and burne it in a panne to
powder and then take a flaxen cloth and make thereof
a pockett and putt it full of the powder and lay it to the
sore tooth this is good for the tooth ache and the ache of
the head. /

For the boneshave. /
Take blossomes of broome hayreve haxhone leaves of Lawrell
waterkernsey Hysope wormewood pellitory red nettles
horehound herbe Ivie by even portions seeth these in his owne
urine and annoynt him against the fire/.

To quench thirst. /
Take the roote of Lovage and Stampe it and temper it with
water and drinke it 3 nights when thou goest to bedd –
and it shall doe away thy thirst. /

For wormes or Trunchions./
Take a porcon of papwort that is called sheetewort and
stampe it and wring out the juyce through a cloth and
give him to drinke. /

For the Emierods. /
Take opium and eurose water and wett a little cotton in
the Eurose and lay it to the sore and it shall heale it. /

For the gowte hott or could. /
Take swines greace and sheepes tallow the dounge of a goose
and a good quantitie of ground Ivie and grinde all these
together and after boyle them and put it in a boxe and annoynt
thee therewith. /

Another. /
For the quicke gowte drinke herbe Ivie. /

For the mygrinne. /
Take rosin frankencense masticke gome eder turpentine
galbanum by even portions and take oyle olive and powder
of Lynseede bayes of Lorell powder of anyes Cumin by
even portions medle them together and make a cap of lether
and lay this thing therein and take a lynnen cloth and lay
it above and binde it well about thine head and that will
last 20 yeares. /

For the Stomacke. /
Take an oz of annys and halfe an oz of galingall and make
thereof powder and drinke at evening luke warme with ale. /

For the Canker.
Take a Cranes head and the feete and the bowells and make
thereof powder and lay it to the Canker and it shall heale
that and all manner of wounds

For fevers and impostems.
Take spinnogre Cullombine turmentill mace felon the seede
of fennell the seede of golds of each of these herbs and of
the seeds; take an handfull and dry them well out of
the sunne and make thereof powder and give it a man
to drinke. /

For the flixe to stop it. /





Take saffron indorted and lay it on the navell and binde
it just to thy navell all the day and the night and it
will stop it. /

Another for the same. /
Take plantane seede and brag it in a mortar and drinke
it with warme ale and it shall stanch if it bee curable. /

For the maras
Take sheepes dounge and powder of Cumin and franke
ensence and make of all these powder then take a bile and
heate it in the fire and cast the powder thereon and stride
over it and use this at morneing and evening and for to alay
the maras smell unto Asafetida. /

A medicine for stopping at the brest. /
Take the pith of the stalke of the red colewort and take an
handfull of parsley roote and an handfull of hysope rootes –
and boyle them in cleere stale ale untill the rootes bee tender
and then put sugar thereto and drinke that at the morning
and at evening. /

A good medicine for scabbs. /
Take frankensencen an oz of cleane greace of barvow
swine 2 oz of gray salt a quarter of an ownce costed
and put thereto a 3d waight of quicksilver mortified
of all these, make an oyntment and annoynt the palmes
of thy hands and the soles of thy feete morneing and
evening. /

A powder for a webb in the eye. /
Take red roses and sugar and salgemine by even portions and make
thereof powder and take a little of that powder and
lay it in the eye and that shall make it whole. /

The tokens of death
Take milke of a woman and drop it in his urine that is

sicke

sicke and if it fleete above hee shall live and if it
sinke hee shall dye of that evill. /

Another for the same. /
Take an handfull of rewe and stampe it with oyle of
roses and lay it above on his head shaven and if hee neese
once hee shall live and if hee neese nott hee shall die. /

Another if hee shall dye in the yeare following
When the partie is lett blood take a little thereof and
put it in a dish full of water and if it sinke all to
the bottome hee shall live and not dye that yeare and
if it departe and fleete above as oyle there is perrill in
that man. /

Another for the same. /
In the first letting blood of the yeare take the man
and lett him doe his blood in a lynnen cloth and wash
it soe longe in the runing water untill the water that
runneth away bee cleere And if that within the
cloth bee as it were white flesh like unto the flesh of
a chicken that man shall live and if it bee blacke hee
shall dye that yeare. /

Another for the same. /
If a sicke man beginne to teare his clothes and looke
much on his fingers hee shall dye and if the teares
rune of a mans right eye and of a woman on the left eye. /

For vomitting of bloode. /
Take ache and mint rew and betaine and seeth them
well in goates milke and drinke it and it helpeth. /

A remedy for one that hath drunke venome or poyson
Take betany and stampe it with water and drinke it
fasting and if thou have drunke venome or poysone
thou shalt cast it out. /

Another





Take betany dry it and make powder thereof and take
of that powder as much as thou canst take betweene
thy 3 fingers at twice and putt thereto a cupfull of wine
and seethe them together untill the third parte bee soden
away and drinke it fastinge. /

To knowe whether a man that is wounded
shall live or dye. /
Take pimpernell stampe it and distemper it with
water and give him to drinke and if hee vomitt hee shall
dye and if hee brooke it hee shall live. /

Another
Take Cinqufolly and give the patient to drinke and if it
remaineth with him hee shall live if not hee shall dye. /

To make a medicine that is good for all
manner of gowtes
Take brockes greace Catts greace hares greace, swines
greace, doggs greace Capons greace, sewett of odour
sheepes tallowe of each a like quantitie and melt them
in a panne, and after that take rubarb the juyce, morrell
bymaline, comfrey dyfis rewe Plantane, Mays harest
macefelon and dragons of each a like quantitie fry
them in a panne with the aforesaid greace and keepe
it well for it is the best oyntment for the gout that
can be made. /

To make unguentum geneste. /
Take flowers of broome and of woodwex and stampe
it with may butter and lett it stand soe all night
and noe more and on the morrowe melt it in a panne
and power it on a cloth and this oyntment is good for
all could gowtes and other could evills and for sleeping
of hands and feete. /

To make a powder for an man that is overcome. /
Take the seede of smallage and bayes and anyseedes Turmentill
the roote of quinfolly and the roote of philipendula Gromell seedes
the seede of saxifrage the seede of stanmarch black seede and
of tylstell powder of Liquorice to sweeten it flower of Canell
flower of gallingale and powder of ginger bruise all these together
in a mortar and use it in thy pottage or in thy drinke. /

A good intret for sores.
Take sheepes tallowe and swines greace and melt them a little
and take and grinde it and put thereto vergin waxe stampe Cumin
and put thereto parrofin and olibanum a little masticke and pitch
and melt them well together, Then take a good quantitie of
saffron and yelkes of eggs grinde them together and lett the
other coole well and put that thereto afterwards and stirr it
well together and use it on leather as thou wilt. /

To make popilion. /
Take and gather leaves of popler and synchon and rybgreace
Plantane and insgmemy penny grasse and morrell both the
greater and the lesse grinde them well, smallage and brooklemp
white popie and other herbes that bee could stampe them with
sheepes tallowe and swines greace and butter and lett them
stand a day or two and then take the juyce of ribgrasse and
morrell and putt it thereto then take sheepes tallow and
swines greace and melt them together and put thereto the
herbes and seeth them and putt butter thereto and when they
have sodde long enough together clense them through a cloth
into a vessell and lett it coole and melt it often, this medicine
is good for all manner of hott sores. /

A drinke for the could dropsie. /
Take of Allisanders lb iii of saven lb ii of rew lb ii of sage
lb i of red docke lb iii of the Crope of and the roote all these
hearbes must bee put into 5 gallons of white wine or els of
red wine and lett it seeth untill the third parte bee wasted
away and after clense it and putt it into a … pott that





neuer had liquour in it before and then giue the sicke
a good draught to drinke in the morneing and another at
euening and therest that shalbee left shalbe put into a
greate newe pott filled with good ale and looke that the sicke
drinke none other drinke vntill hee bee whole likewise
hee must abstaine from all salt meates./

For the hott dropsie./.
Take 9 greate wormes and cut of all their heads and grinde
the wormes and temper them with water and giue the
sicke to drinke euery day a worme and put therein sugar
if thou haue it or els liquorice to make it sweete and
it behooveth the sicke to abstaine from goose flesh and
onyons and from all vnwholesome meates and drinkes

For the dropsie hott or could./
Take yonge growne elders and shaue of the greene rinde
aboue and then shaue the greene rinde beneth the quantity
of 2 handfulls and lett it boyle in a gallon of good stale ale
vntill it come to a pottle and then giue the sicke to
drinke and it shall doe away all malady./

Another for the same./.
Take a hoggs mawe and cutt it and lay it hott vpon the
memberes and hee shall pisse out his malady this is
as goode for a woman as for a man./.

For the palsie
Take Cowslips and leaues of primerose and sage leaues and
leaues of red nettles and mustard and grinde them all
well together and after clenseth through a lynnen
cloth and putt it in a boxe and annoynt the sicke./.

A remedy for a disease in a mans stomacke./
Take auence and any seede and boyle them together in wine
till it bee halfe boyled away after the portion of the
wine that is putt thereto more or lesse and lett him drinke
it as hott as hee may suffer it and if the drinke bee bitter
thou



thou mayst putt therein sugar or liquerice to make it sweete./.

For the Te sicke./
Take the milke of a woman and drinke it last at night as
hott as thou mayst suffer it put sugar therein drinke the same
9 dayes or more if it bee rooted and it will heale thee./.

Another for the same./.
Take the roote of horshelne an handfull and the quantitie of
the third parte of liquorice and bruise it and after boyle it
well together in a gallon of water vntill it come to a pottle
and lett the sicke drinke thereof could morneing and
euening.

For the quotidian axes.
Take rew wormewood dandilion herbe Ive and sage and
30 cornes of peper breade them and mingle them and giue
the sicke to srinke with wine or ale before his goeing foorth
and after hee hath drunke the medicine hee shall couer himselfe
with clothes and hee shall sleepe and escape./.

A remedy against the flixe./
Take milke of almons made with raspis or els with strong
red wine and put it into a vessell and when the sicke listeth
to assay this medicine take a portion thereof and seeth it
and when it is sodden take blank that bee a month ould
at the least and put therein and lett them seethe soke and
lett the sicke vse this medicine as hott as hee may suffer
it and hee shalbe whole within 3 dayes if hee shall liue
but hee must vse noe other thinge therewith but this
medicine./.

Another./
Take a stoole that is siege that is closed all about and
take an hott glowing tile and lay it vnder the siege and
then take a quarter of a pound of raw lence powdred







and lay it on the tyle and set the sicke aboue on the siege
and putt clothes aboute him that there may goe noe ayre
foorth but euen into the fundament and lay another
tyle in the fire that may heate while the other cooleth
and thus serue him well as longe as an oz lasteth which
is for 4 times and after that is spent make a fayre fire
of 3 or 4 Charcoales that hee may suffer well the heate
and lett him sate of an hott blank and red wine warme
and none other drinke and serue him thus 3 dayes and
hee shalbe staunched fairly And if it bee bloody flixe
lett him drinke 3 dayes 3 penny waight of towne Cressen
seede and 3d waight of hencresse seede that is to say
each day a jd waight of the one and the other medled together
and if the sicke bee curable this will helpe./.

Another./
Take an handfull of henneoreseen and another of plantane
and grinde them in a mortar and fry them well together
with sheepes tallowe and frankensence and make a
plaster thereof and lay to his nauell as hott as hee may
suffer it and lett him vse the siege as aforesaid./.

A remedy against paine in a mans side /
Take hilwort Allisander parseley Louage red fennell
smallage burnett Gromill of each a like quantitie and
seeth them in white wine vntill the halfe bee wasted
away then straine them and lett the sicke drinke thereof
first and last at night hott and in the morneing could ./.

A remedy for paine in the milt
Take the sowthistle and seeth it in white wine and giue the
sicke to drinke first and last and lett him bloode on the
right hand on the vaine betweene the fingers for
on that veine many Ladyes doe lett them blood to haue
a good stomacke to their meate ./.



For the stich or imposteme in any place
Take the roote of the holyoke wash it cleane and seeth
it till it bee tender then power out the water into a
vessell and take lynseede and finegreeke of each a like
quantitie but looke that you haue twice as much of them
as of the roote when it is soden by waight and seeth
the linseede and the fengreeke in the same water that
the holyoke was sodden in right well vntill it bee like
bird lyme, and that the same liquor bee almost sodden
away then stampe the roote and put thereto barly meale
and meddle them well together and fry them with
barrows greace and lay the plaster to the sore as hott as
the sicke may suffer it and within 9 plasters hee shall
bee whole./

For a mans moold that is fallen downe
Take leaues of egrimony a good quantitie wash and
grind them and put thereto a good quantitie of honey
and fry them well together then shaue the head as
farr as the plaster shall lye and lay the plaster on the
moold as hott as the sicke may suffer it ./

Another.
Take Celondine and stampe it with Maybutter and
fry them well together and streine them through a
Canvas and putt it in a boxe it is good to annoynt the
moold therewith when the plaster hath raysed vp
the same againe./.

To purge the stomacke of Evill humours
Take betony Marubium horehounde sage and ginger
and the seede of dyll of each a like quantitie and put
thereto peper and honey stampe it all together and
temper it with wine and giue the sicke to drinke./







To make a man to cast B.
Take the iuyce of stone cropp and drinke it with ale/.

Anothere/.
Take the roote of celondine malue and spurge seede stampe them together
then temper them well with ale and lett it stande a night
and in the morneing seeth it, and after drinke thereof as
thou thinketh good./.

A medicine for all manner of diseases in a
mans body./.
Take 3 crops of hysope 3 crops of sage royall 3 crops of wormwood
3 crops of rewe 3 crops of Centory 3 crops of vervine and bruise
all these together and medle them with good stronge ale
and take halfe a spoonfull of this liquour and put thereto
a quantitie of triacle and drinke it morneing and euening
for the space of 8 dayes or els mae them in powder and eate
them with triacle and thou shalt haue ease./.

A remedie for one that is broken./
Take womans milke Linseede sheepes tallowe polipody
Confery and wheate branne fry all these together and make
a plaster thereof and lay it to the sore as hott as hee may
suffer it./.

A medicine for a sore side./.
Take vinegar sower bread wormewood mint and sheepes greece
and bruise them together fry them in a panne and put
them in a cloth with the medicine about the sore side and
it shall helpe thee./.

A plaster to breake a sore and to rott it./.
Take fengreeke, and mellilocum honey suckle and Linseede and
mint seeth them well in water and make a plaster thereof
and lay it thereto and it shall rott it./.

A remedy for burneing or scalding./.



Take the inner barke of the eller tree and seeth it with oyle
or fresh blood after that putt therein gumme or incense and waxe and
seeth it againe vntill it bee made soft and then annoynt
the burneing and it helpeth./.

Another for scalding./.
Take a spoone full of oyle and 3 drops of water and chafe it
together till it bee thicke as butter and annoynt thee
therewith./.

Another for burneing.
Take ground Ivie and water betony and seeth them in
running water and lay it thereto as hott as thou may suffer it/.

To purge the stomacke and the wombe./.
Rec. sene vne ij turtit electo gingeb. mundati an vnc
panis sucare vne. vna istam pulvarizentur et cu lacte
craprimo sum antur vnum cochare tin et sicolea duetur
in receptione sumet rubarbam dragma vna et pulvarizentur
in premissis et ponatur de pulvare predicto et mittat vnum
cocliare in suo potagio et sic sumat semel septimana
tin et bupurgabit stomacum et ventreet quatour humores
seu complexiones./.

For the bone shawe
Take balme And fetherfewe 2 partes balme the third parte
fetherfew stampe them and temper them with stale ale
and lett the sicke drinke thereof first and last make
a plaster of fetherfewe and of popler leaues and of may
butter the 2 partes popler leaues and the 3 partes
fetherfewe stampe them well and putt thereon a
good quantitie of buter meddle them well together
and lett the sicke annoynt him well against a fire made
of Charcoales as hott as hee may suffer it an make a plaster
of flowes of broome stamped and oyle and well fryed together







as hott as hee may suffer it./.

For one that spitteth bloode./.
Take betony verven milfollie and quinfollie of each
a like quantitie wash them and grind them in a mortar
and straine out the iuyce and putt thereto as much goates
milke and boyle them together very well and lett the
sicke drinke thereof vij dayes in the waxeing of
the moone and vij in the waneing of the moone
and lett him drinke of mund and Comfrey 9 dayes with
stale ale and hee shalbe whole./.

For him that Casteth bloode./.
Take ache mynt ambrose rew and betony of each a like
quantitie and boyle them in good milke and drinke it
3 dayes and bee whole./.

Another./.
Take pilioll and make powder thereof and eate it
with an egge 3 dayes./.

For one that hath noe appetite to his meate./.
Take Centory and seeth it well in stale ale and when
it is well sodden stampe it well and putt it againe into
the pott and seeth it well then clense it through a
cloath and take 2 partes of the iuyce and the third parte
of honey and boyle it together and putt it in a boxe
and giue the sicke to eate thereof euery day till hee bee
well and it shall doe away the glett from his hearte
and cause him to haue a good appetite to his meate./.

Another for the same./.
Take Centory Plantane and peper and seeth them with
wine and drinke them when thou goest to bedd./.

Another./
Take the iuyce of fennell the 2 partes and honey the
third./.



third parte and seeth them together in a panne to the thicknesse
of the honey and put peper thereto and take euery morneing
a spoonefull

To knowe whether a wounded man shall liue or dye./.
Take the iuyce of terfolly or of pimpernell and giue him to drinke
and if hee cast it vp hee is not curable for it is not curable a signe
of death, And if hee keepe it hee is curable then giue him to
drinke these 3 herbs 3 dayes. pimpernell Bugle and and stynakell
and they shall come through the wounbde and purge the wound
of bloode but looke if it bee on the head and if the panne bee
hurt giue him noe sinakell for it peirceth the braine./.

Another./
Take white lettice and giue him to drinke and if hee hould it
hee shall liue and if hee cast it hee shall dye./.

Another./.
Take mowse eare stampe it and temper it with stale ale
and giue him to drinke and if hee cast hee shall die els not

A medicine for a wounde/.
Take the heads of leekes with all the leaues and stampe
them and putt the iuyce to the wounde and take heards
and make thereof a tent and wett it in the iuyce and
put it into the wounde and the substance of the leekes
that the iuyce was wrung out of ley them vpon the wound
and binde them thereto; lett this plaster lye thereto the
space of 3 dayes and remoue it not aboue once a day at
the most, and after 3 dayes take wheate meale and good
wine and swines greace and boyle them together Then
take a lynnen cloth and put it 2 or 3 double or els take
heards or white lether and put thy plaster thereon
and lay it on the wounde and doe soe euery day till it
be whole, and euery day giue him to drinke these 3
herbs.







herbs Bugill Ligill and synakell and when hee hath drunke
these herbs they shall come out at the wounde and shall
Clense it within and heale it without./.

Another./
Take Centory and make powder thereof and strowe it vpon
the wounde and it healeth./.

A remedy for a veine that is cutt a sunder./
Take burnt lyme and make powder thereof and take
honey and the whites of eggs, stampe them together
and on the sore lay flaxe and put thereon the plaster
and take it not away vntill the third day and when thou
takest it away wett it with the sicke mans water
for to lighten the plaster and lett it remaine for if
thou drawe it away it will bleede againe./.

Another./.
Take rewe and seeth it in water and stampe it and lay
thereto and take vnwashed lambs wolle and lay it aboue
and it shalbee whole./.

To asswage the ache of the wounde./.
Take nepp and stampe it and temper it with wine and
giue him to drinke and it shall doe away the ache, if
the wound bee shutt together take bryan that is the
wilde neppe and make powder thereof and lay to the
wound and it shall open it.

To stanch bloode./
Take broome and shaue the rinde of with a knife and
make balls thereof and putt in the wounde and binde it
with a cloth./.

Another
Take salt and burne it in a sherd and make powder
thereof and putt it into the wounde./.



For bleeding at the nose/
Take Plantane and drinke the iuyce thereof and it
shall stanch the bleeding./.

To auoyde bloude out of a man that hath a wound
or bruise./.
Take the iuyce of nepp and drinke it and it shall cast
out the bloode./.

Another./
Take white mallowes and make powder thereof and it
shall cast out bloode./

A medicine for the healing of wounds./
Take the iuyce of alder and the yelke of an egg and honey
and wheate meale and take as much of the iuyce as aboute
the quantitie of 2 yelkes and one blank of an egg and of
the soft about the midst of the g egg and afterwards of
the meale soe that all bee medled together and thereof
make a plaster; Alsoe lett it bee well medled and very much
with honey and annoynt the cloth and after that putt it
vpon the sickenesse euery wounde is healed in this manner./.

A remedy for a worme that is crept into a
mans eare./.
Take the iuyce of Louage and lett him hould it in his eare a
little while and it shall kill the wormes and make them to
come out of the head./.

For a woman that hath lost her milke./.
Take the flower of white thorne with the leaues stampe
it and temper it with what liquour thou wilt and lett the
woman drinke it and her milke shall come againe And
if it bee in other season of the yeare take the launcetts
of the white thorne and cut them and bruise them into
powder lett her drinke that and her milke shall come againe./.

A remedy for a legg that is full of holes./.
Take thine owne vrine purified and put thereto mallowes
after midsomer, the leaues and the rootes and sheepes
tallowe and boyle it till it bee thicke and hott lay it on a cloth







and binde it aboute thy legge as hott as though mayst suffer
it in the morneing at noone and at night /.

Another. /
Take mallowes mugweede docke rootes and leaves and boyle
them together untill they bee tender and as hott as thou
mayest suffer it bynde it to the sore legge and doe soe untill it
bee whole

A remedy for any ach or swelling in a mans body. / .
Take rewe and lovage and stampe it with honey and lay it to
thine evill it it shall doe away thine ache. /

Another for the same. / .
Take the roote of walwort and seeth it in water and then
take it and doe away the over rinde and take the middle rinde
and stampe it with boares greace and doe it in a cloth and
binde it thereto.

Another.
Take the red water cresses and shred them smale and put them
in a pott and thicke drests therewith and fresh sheepes tallow
and lett it seeth till it bee thicke then take a cloth that will
cover the swelling and put the plaster upon the cloth and binde
it aboute all the swelling and lett it remaine there all night and
it shall asswage the swelling. / .

Another. /
Take gromell and seeth it well and take sengreene and sheepes
tallowe and put thereto and put it in a cloth and binde it about
the evill and it shall helpe. /.

Another. /
Take the docke roote and seeth it well in water and then take
it up and stempe it and then put it into a panne and sheepes
tallowe thereto and stirr it till the tallowe bee melted and put it
in a cloth and lay it to the sore and it helpeth. /

Another.
Take mugwort and stampe it with ould greace and lay it to the
soles of thy feete. /

Another.
Take Rye meale 2 partes and the meale of the redd lentill
the 3rd parte and seeth it in a pott with Eysell and honey and
lay thereto. /

Another./
Take wormewood and seeth it in oyle olive or els of lynseede
and straine it through a cloth and put it in a boxe and annoynt
thee therewith and it shall doe away the paine. /

Another.
Take mallowes and stampe them, ould greace and ley it thereto
and it shall draw out the ache. /

To make a pretious water for sore eyes. /
Take fennell, rew, eufrus, vervin turmentill, betany red rose
daysies, Ivie that is called oculus christi Chekweede pimpernell
Celandine, Sylage, feltwort, vyne leaves Smallage Egremony
and woodbine of each a like quantitie and stampe them a
little and lay them the first day in white wine the second
day in a man childs urine, of a mayds the third day in
womans milke the 4th day in good honey and then still them and
keepe the water in a glasse this is a pretious water for all
evills in the eyes. /

For quotidian axes.
Take rosemary sage and thyme and onsett gold and unsett
porett and balme and shred them smale and seeth them in good
ale dreggs and put thereto faire wheate bread crumes and make
it thicke and plaster it on a lynnen cloth and lay it to his heart
for 24 howers and soe serve him 4 dayes and hee shalbe whole.





A drinke for the same medicine
Take white wine and ginger and chaffe it by the fire and
drinke it as hott as thou canst suffer it. /

Another. / .
Take Liquoras, graines, Saffron, Cloves and mace and seeth
Them in ould ale and put thereto Liquerice and drinke the same
morneing and evening. /

To drawe out a thorne out of a mans foote. / .
Take the barke of a hathorne and bruise it well in a mortar and
seeth it in red wine untill one halfe bee sodden away then power
out thy wine and cast the barke into a mortar and grinde it
smale and temper it with boares greace and fry them together
and make a plaister and Lay it to the sore as hott as the patient
may suffer it. / .

For a disease called Nolome tangere. /
Take the greace of a Capon or of a henne and putt thereto powder
of unflecked lyme or powder of peper doe for 3 or 4 times
and at the last doe thereto powder of toads stooles untill the
sore bee dead that it may wynn that which before was lost. /

A good plaster for the fester. /
Take plantane and white tansy and                       Centinodie
Swines greace ache and avence stampe every herbe by it selfe
and wringe out the juyce and looke that thou have of every
herbe a like quantitie and that it bee thicke then take
the juyce of walwort and stampe it and take as much juyce
of it as thou hast of all the other herbs and when it is thicke
then take virgins waxe and fresh sheepes tallowe honey May
butter ould swines greace and wine of each a like quantitie and
lett it bee thicke and that all the things aforesaid bee alsoe as
much as all the juyce of walworte putt all these things in a panne
except the white mallowe by it selfe alone and boyle them all

together

together and then boyle the white mallowe by it selfe thou
must not wring the juyce of it out as thou dost of the other
herbs when it is well boyled wringe it out through a cloth
and put it in boxes, Then take wheate flower and put a parte
of the oyntment thereto and boyle it together untill it bee thicke
as pap then wash thy wound morneing and evening with
white wine and lay then thy plaster thereon and doe for every
day till it bee whole and every day fasting give him to drinke
wormewood and avence. /

A good powder to kill the fester. / .
Take branes and Rye and Ivory and arnement and salt of each
a like quantitie and put it all in a newe pott and burne it
till thou mayst stampe it or grinde it all to powder and straine
it through a boulting cloth then take the stalke of a mallowe
and wett it in honey and take up of the powder that cleaveth
to the stalke and put it in a hole downe to the grounde and lay
the plaster thereon that is before said and this powder will
kill the fester and foe the steale within with the powder 5
dayes or 7 nights and after that put a tent of flaxe therein
and even as the sore healeth make the tent lesser and shorter
and every day twice wash it with white wine. /

To know the could fester from the hott
The could fester is with smale holes and the hott fester is
with broade holes. /

A remedy for the Could fester. /
Take the juyce of avence or els the juyce of Lan royall and
the white of an egge of each a like quantitie and take Rye
flower and kneade it therewith and put it in the holes as
it weare a plaster and binde it thereto with a cloth and
lett it lye there on till it fall of it selfe and then put others

to





to it and ever as they fall of putt others thereto and soe
Continue untill it bee whole and lett the patient every day
drinke the juyce of avence. /

For the burneing festar /
Take the flower of Rye and cleane honey thereto and make
hard dough thereof and make as many little Cakes as there bee holes
and put upon every cake a  hole a Cake and when they bee wett
Take them away and Lay others thereto and doe thus untill
it bee whole and lett the patient for the time eate noe quafie
meate or drinke. /

A remedy for the Canker. /
Take lungwort and dry it and make powder thereof and lay
it on the Canker. /

Another. /
Take water of Cheverfole that is the honysuckle of the hedge
and that will kill the Cankar in the mouth or in any other
place in the body. /

Another for the same. /
Take the heade of a storke the feete and the bowells and put
them in a pott that never was used and put that pott into
an oven and dry it soe that thou mayst make powder thereof
take of that powder and lay it to the Canker and within few
dayes it shalbe whole. /

A rememdy for wormes in a mans body. /
Take and gather in the mounth of May all these herbes of every
one a like Sage mint scabiose, Spigernell, avence, Enfrace
Verven, Betany with all the leaves and the rootes and put
them in an oven to dry or els in the sunne and when they bee
dry rub the leaves betweene thy hands to powder and stampe
the rootes to powder and give the patient to drinke for all the
evills heere under written, for the palsie, for the goute for the
menison, for the felon, for the squimacie, for an imposteme, for venome for
poyson, for dropsie for headache for wormes in a mans body. /

For deafnes of the head. /
Take onyons and make them hould in the manner of a pott
then take oyle olive and milke of a man childe and the urine
of a boare and the greace of a blacke eele and the powder of
Annyes and the juyce of poretts as much of the one as of the
other by even portions. Then fill the onions of all these
things then medle them all together and roste the onyons
and when they bee well rosted, then stampe them all together
in a dish, after that put them in a cleane basen soe that
the bason bee gilded, and lett it lye a night and a day and the
oyle will goe out from them then take that oyle and keepe
it in a glasse and put thereof in the eare of him that is
sicke when hee goeth to bedd or to sleepe and within short
time hee shalbe whole. /

For paine in the mouth or in the throate. /
Take the five leaved grasse and boyle it well in water and
when it is well boyled, hould thy mouth over that pott scume
them well, then sup of that water and hould it in thy
mouth untill it bee could and then cast it out and sup more
and soe the third time use this 3 dayes and you shalbe whole. /

Another. /
Take the juyce of sage or the juyce of primrose and put
it in thy mouth and it will helpe you. /

Another. /
Take ach mint rewe and Betany and boyle them together
in good milke. /

Another.
Take pilioll and make powder thereof and put of that powder
into honey and eate it 3 dayes. /

For an arme that is rankled with blood letting. /
Take Rye flower and the juyce of lillie and make paste thereof

and





and make thereof 2 smale Cakes and bake them in the
embers and take the one and Lay to the sore and as hott as
the patient may suffer it and when that is could take
the other and doe soe untill it bee cured. /

To cure wounds that bee rankled
Take frankensence and wheate flower and the juyce of
walwort and of ache and of pettimorell and of hemlocke
and take barrowes greace and fry them together and make
a plaster and Lay to the wounde and Lay a could leafe betweene
and it shall cease the achinge and asswage the swellinge. /

Another /
Take heads of leekes with the leaves and bruise them in a mortar
and put thereto wheate flower and honey of each a like much
and fry them together, and make a plaster thereof and
Lay to the wounde and it shall stint the swelling and acking. /

A remedy for wounds that bee some healed
and sore under. /
Take the                 of the goate and boyle them in vinegar till
it bee sodden and stampe them in a mortar, and put thereto
honey of perofin and frankensence medle them well together
and fry them with barrowes greace, and make a plaster and
lay it to the sore somewhatt warme and that will open the
sore and drawe out the filth therein and heale up the wounde. /

Another. /
Take egrimony and betany and the roote of lillie of each a
like much and stampe them well together and put thereto
honey and the white of an egge and Rye meale and medle
them well together and make a plaster and lay to the sore
it will open it and drawe out the filth bee it from wood
thorne or any other filth whatsoever. /

A remedy for wounds that bee full of blood
Take the red nettle stampe it and temper it with vinegar
and Lay to the wound and it will doe away the bloode and
make the wound cleane. /

Another. /
Take the leaves of henbane bruise them and doe thereto
May butter, stampe them and boyle them well together and
strayne them through a cloth and put thereof into the wounde
it will drawe out the bloode and clense the wounde and if
the wound bee deepe in the flesh, drinke asmund and Comfrey
and if the veine bee cutt drinke spurge. /

A remedy for a fester. /
Take honey and powder of verdegreace and seeth them together
in an earthen vessell and if thou wilt know when it is
enough take a drop with thy slice and drop it on a could Iron
or on a could stone and if it bee hard when it is could then it
is enough then fill the wounds with that salve twice on a
day and that will heale the festar men call this red salve. /

A drinke for the festar. /
Take avence archangell, heyone, betany and vervine of each
a like much heyone take thereof most boyle them with wine
and lett the sicke drinke thereof first and last. /

A rememdy for the Canker and the goute festar. /
Take the juyce of rewe and of mints honey and vinegar of
each a like much and meddle them together sett them on a
soft fire and boyle them softely and stirr them with a slice
when it              as bl pitch and Lay it to the sore as hott as
the sicke may suffer it and use this medicine untill hee bee whole. /

A rememdy for the mygrin. /
Take an handfull of Chickemeate an handfull of red fennell
an handfull of wormewood and a quantitie of white wood
wine and grinde them together with a little triacle and

wash





wash thy temples therewith and make a plaster of
the same herbs and lay to the disease as hott as the patient
may suffer it/ .

Another for the same. /
Take polipodie that groweth upon the walls or upon
stones or upon the oake and alume and bruise them together
and temper them with vinegar and make a plaster and lay
it upon the disease and another upon the necke of the same.

A rememdy for all manner of diseases of aches
in the head. /
Take 3 pennywaight of the roote of pellitory of spaine
and halfe a pennywaight of spicknard grinde them together
in good vinegar and take a sawcer full of honey and 5 sawcer
full of mustard seede and meddle them well together and
lett the sicke use thereof a spoonefull at once and hould it
in his mouth as longe as a man may say 2 Creedes then
spitt it out into a vessell and then take another and doe
this x or xii times a good while after thou hast at noone
and a little before night as many times and when thou art
ready to goe to bed wash thy mouth cleane and drinke a
draught of what liquour thou wilt use this medicine
3 dayes and hee shalbe whole. /

A powder for the same. /
Take halfe an ouce of galingale and an oze of ginger
…oz of nutmeggs a quarter of an ounce of cloves and a
pennyworth of flower of Canell a pennyworth of spicknard
and half a pennyworth of anyseeds a quarter of an ounce
of elecampana doz of liquorice and an ounce of sugar
beate them all to powder and meddle them well together
and use this first and last a spoonefull at once and thou
shalt within 4 dayes bee whole. /

A medicine for all manner of evill for of
the eyes
Take these xii herbs Stamarch, fennell, rewe, vervaine

Egremony

Egrimony Betany Celondine Trefolly, that the male is Camedrios that
is Enfras pimpernell sage of every one a handfull stampe them smale
and lett the juyce runne through a cloth and keepe it for the eyes. /

Another for the pearle. /
Take white ginger and rub it on a whetstone of Norway put
the ginger into white wine then put the white wine into a pewter
sawcer and sett the one end of the whetstone in the sawcer and
soe rubbe thy face of ginger upon thy whetstone into thy wine
then take a fether and dip it into the liquour and therewith
wash the sore and that shall doe away the pearle and save the eye. /

Another for the webbe. /
Take the gall of an hare and honey putrified of each a like much
and medle them together and with a fether lay it on the webbe
and that shall breake it within 3 nights ./

A rememdy for wormes in the body.
Take betony and savin dry them and make powder thereof lett
the sicke drinke of that powder with hott water and as soone as
the powder is in the body it will kill them and bringe them out. /

Another. /
Take singreene and woderon and fetherfoy stampe them and
drinke the juyce with warme wine and it will kill them and
brings them out. /

To kill tronchions in a mans body. /
Take a sawcer full of the juyce of Calamint and a sawcer
full of the juyce of Centory a sawcer full of honey a sawcer
full of vinegar and a spoonefull of salt and medle thy liquor
together and looke that they bee salt boyle them a little over
the fire and put them in a cleane vessell and lett the sicke
use this medicine fastinge a sawcer full at … on …
morneing at noone and againe at night the same day that hee
taketh this medicine lett him bee fasting …ne. /





A remedy for sore fingers
Take 5 or 6 red coale leaves and put therein leaves of
sorrell or the smale branches and winde them together and
put therein the embers and rose them tender, then take them
out of the leaves, and thou shalt finde therein a faire salve binde
it to the sore and thou shalt bee whole. /

A rememdy for sore leggs and rawe. /
Take the malve leaves and the greate Cleete shred them
smale and seeth them with Coales of one yeare seeth
them till they bee as thicke as pape make thereof a plaster
and lay it on the sore and take a whelps of the first litter
and burne him in a pott to powder and cast of that powder
upon the sore and it shalbe whole. /

A remedy for a man or woman that is blasted
Take ould stale ale boyle it and scome it well and take Rye meale
with pisse and mingle all these together in the manner of a
plaster and spread it abroade upon his face that is blasted.
when hee goeth to bedd and hee shalbe whole. /

Another
Take a henns egge and rost it hard and doe away the yelke
take the white and put it in a brasen morter and put thereto
a quarter of an ounce of Coperas and grinde them well together
that it bee all soe smale as oyntment and annoynt the face of
the sicke and it shall cease the ache and doe away the swelling
and when the face is nigh whole annoynt the sicke with a
little popilion and that shall make the skyn whole and soft. /

A medicine for Costivenes
Take the roote of polipody that groweth on the oke wash it
stampe it and temper it with white wine and lett it stand all
night and on the morrow straine it and drinke it and that
shall make deliverance in a short time. /

To make a sopositorie
Take a sawcer full of honey that is purified and a handfull of

salt

salt and fry them well together untill they be blacke and
take a cleane bason and fayre lard and annoynt the bason
bottome with the larde, Then forme the suppository in the bason
bottome while it is hott. /

A medicine for swelling wombes. /
Take greene rewe and give the sicke to drinke with wine or
ale and hee shalbe whole. /

A rememdy for akeing of the wombe. /
Take tansie rewe and sotherwood and eate it with salt
and it will helpe. /

To knowe when a man is stroken on the head
with a staffe whether the panne bee broken or
not and the flesh whole. /
Take a rasor and shave the head where the sore is and take
a cleane Lynnen cloth double and the white of an egge and spread
it on the cloth and Lay it on the sore all night when the sicke
goeth to bedde and binde it thereto untill the morneing then
take it away and if the pan bee broken it wilbe moyst
Then thou must cut the flesh against the sore and lett out
the bruised blood and put in some oyntment that is good for
it and soe thou shalt save him and heale the wounde other
wise it were not possible for the man to live. /

A remedy for a paine in the mouth. /
Take salt Cumyn and peper of each a like quantitie make
powder thereof and give the sicke to drinke with hott water
with a spoone. /

To make Oleum Benedictum. /
Take Turbut 1 oz of dragons of roses v oz of Crowsocke
iii oz 5 pennywaight of Saxifrage, handfull of Ambrose
Sperma ceti halfe an oz Salgeme 2 oz galingale 2 oz
maces 2 oz Caraway 2 oz of fennell 2 oz of spurge 2 oz

and





and of mader 2 oz. and all that needeth to bee stamped
stampe them and all that needeth to bee melted melt
them then take all these herbs and rootes and lay them in
fuse xiij dayes and as many nights with a gallon of oyle
oliue and halfe a pinte of oyle de bay and then take it vp
out of the fuse and warme them all a little and then straine
them and melt sweet may butter and putt thereto and this
is good for all euills in mans body./.

To make vnguentum agrippa./.
Take halfe a pounde of honey one pound of oyle oliue halfe
a pound of may butter a quarte of masticke and boyle all
together a little while and lokke thou haue in fuse a vence
rew hysope Betany occulus Christi vervin red nettles
Plantant bugill Con milfoly grana dei morsus diaboli
egrimony red roses Consope eglenter Seruce the blanke and
when they bee ready put thereto a quarter of Turpentine./.

To make salue for butches or biles.
Take parrofin and white incense and sheepes tallow molten
of each a like much melt them together and stirr them
well vntill they bee medled straine them in a vessell and
put thereto the iuyce of the roote of walwort as much of
white as of the other and medle them together and make
a plaster thereof./.

To make a seare cloath./
Take halfe a quarter of vergin waxe d quarter of Turpentine
d quarter of parrofin d quarter of powder of Cumin and
an oz of powder of Bayes an ounce of frankencence 1oz
of oyle oliue 1 oz of dew 1 oz of popilion take all these things
except the powder sett them on the fire and stirr them vntill
they bee molten then sett them downe and skume them not
nor straine them not but stirre them fast vntill they bee
nigh could then strowe thy powders with thine owne hande
soft and easilie and stirr with the other hand vntill thou
haue



haue strowed in all thy powders together euen as thou wouldest
strowe flower into thy childs pap when it is all in and
almost could spreade it with thy sticke on a white leathere
then take white lynnen cloth and lay it aboue and couch
it fast thereto and sewe the seelings together./.

To make popilion
Take 4 pound of popler leaues and 3lb of herbe water 1lb of
henbane 1lb of pettimorrell 1lb of orpin 1lb of singreene
d lb of weybred dlb of endiue dlb of violetts dlb of well grasse
wash them cleane stampe them and doe thereto 2lb and a
halfe of Barrow greace melted and purified and when they
bee well medled put them in a pott and close it and lett it stand
9 dayes then worke it vp straine it and sett it on the fire
a little and then straine it an put it in a boxe this is popilion./

A pretious oyntment called narvaile which is
exceeding good for synnewes./.

Take ambrose Camomile Betany sage mynt hey one Sother
wood mugwort wormewood well cresses mallowes hollihoke
horehound and red nettles lorrell leaues walwort of each
halfe a quarter wash them cleane and stampe them and
put thereto halfe a pound of may butter and stampe it with
thine herbes and put thereto a quarter of oyle de oliue and
meddle them well together and put them all in an earthen
pott and couer it and soe let it stand 9 dayes then take it out
of the pott and put it in a panne and sett it on the fire and
fry it well and stirre it well with a slice to the bottome
that it burne not, when it is hott take it downe and
straine it into a vessell and sett that liquiur ouer the fire
againe and put thereto halfe a quarter of waxe in smale
peeces and a quarter of weathers tallowe melted and purified
and boyle them a little then doe thereto a quarter of frankensence
well







well powdred and meddle them together and stirre it well
till it bee relented then take it downe and straine it and
lett it coole then cutt it and lett it out out of the water then
stirre it and clense it on the nether side and doe away
the filth and sett it ouer the fire againe vntill it bee
melted and with a fether skume it an put it in boxes
this is the right workeing of Nervaile./.

Vnguentum veride

Take the roote and leafe of Celondine and the roote of Colts
foote and the leaues of Scabiose and flos campi of each an
handfull wash them cleane and stampe them and putt thereto j ld of
wethers tallowe another of oyle de oliue and meddle them
well together in a mortar and put them in to an earthen
pott well closed aboue that there may noe ayre come out
and sett it in a moyst place and lett it stande 9 dayes
and after take it out of the pott and put it into a faire
panne and sett it on a Charcoale fire and fry it well
and stirr it ouer the fire againe and doe thereto half a
quarter of vergin waxe in smale peeces and when it is
melted doe thereto 1lb of frankensence half an oz of
masticke another of verdegreace well powdred stirre all
these three together vntill they bee decided then sett them
downe and put thereto halfe an oz of alum epabicke well
powdred and desolued in warme oyle this oyntment is good
for all wounds and to doe away dead flesh and to make newe

An oyntment called vade mecum
Take the iuyce of Celondine and fumiter of borage and
of Scabiose and of the red docke of each a quarter Lytarge
Ceruse burnt of each halfe an ounce and Medle them
with lees of vinegar and barrows greace of each a like
much



much and sett them on the fire and when they seeth cast
in the powders and stirre them together with a slice then
take them downe and doe it into boxes this oyntment is
good for scabbs and many other things

An oyntment called versilium the vertue
whereof is good for many things as to cure
wounds or burnes of any parte of mans body
and for many other infirmities./

Take halfe an oz of Lytarge and halfe a pounde of masticke
and halfe a pounde of Ceruse afor OR blacke plums an oz of
Comfrey halfe an ounce of Calamint and medle them with
euerose and take half an lb of vergin waxe cutt it in smale
peeces and put it in a faire panne and put all these things
thereto and sett it on a Charcoale fire and lett them seeth
till all thy things bee relented and medled alwayes stirr
them with a slice, then take it downe and stirr it vntill
it bee could./

To make a dalue to heale all kinde of woundes
and to knitt sinnewes and vaines that are cut
and alsoe to knitt bones that are broken./.

Take Betony Bugill Sanykill, pimpernell, Milfoyle
Strawberryies, opin sage mouse eare ambrose or
amirose tansey Sotherwood herb Robart herbe water
egrimony plantane sowseck auence madyr consowde
Comfrey osmund Coperas of red coale cropps of hempe
crops of red bryar Crops of red nettles Take thereof by
waight as much as of the other herbes and wash thine
herbs cleane and stampe them smale and take May butter
and if thou canst gett noe butter of euening milke take
butter of ewes milke of the yellowest that thou canst
finde for it is better then the white and take to a quarte of
butter







butter halfe a quarter of vergin waxe and lett thy
butter melt and doe thereto thy waxe when they bee
melted straine them through a smale lynnen cloth then
is thy butter clarified for the waxe sheadeth the butter
and the cordes vntwined then lett thy butter coole and
when it is could take and doe thereto 3lb of thine herbs
halfe a pounde of butter cast them into a mortar and stampe
them together till thou canst see nothing of the butter
take a new earthen pott and putt thine herbes therein
and euer as thou medlest thy butter and them together
and thus porcon thy butter and thy herbs and euer as
thou dost then in the pott knead them downe fast with
thy handes that it bee not hollowe for goeing in of wormes
and when all is in the pott close the pott well aboue that
there may noe ayre goe out and sett it in a moyst place xiij
dayes or 9 at the least and then will it bee hoare aboue then
take it out and put it into a faire panne and sett it ouer
the fire and fry them well together and turne it with
a slice and when it is hott enough take it downe and
presse it and lett it stand vntill it bee could then cutt it
and lett out the water and melt it newe and then put it
into the vessell and take of the same as thou needest.

To make an oyntment which is called Colon
that is good for sore eyes./.

Take a bason and scoure it well and annoynt the bason
within with Lard of Bacon and vpon the lard annoynt
it with honey soe that the honey may goe ouer the bacon
on euery side halfe an inche and then take a new pott full

A medicine against the frensie./

Take 2 handfull of mans mother wort and put it vpon his head
or vnder his Chynne or his throate and binde it strongly by
the space of xv dayes or 9 at the least and hee shalbe whole./

Another.

Take ground Ivie and seeth it in water then take the leaues
and stampe them and put thereto eysell and euerose and put it
in a cloth and binde it about his head and it shall cause him to sleepe./

To make Turpentine for entreat./.

Take 3 quarters of cleane rosin a quarter of parrofin halfe a
pounde of good oyle that is greene and make powder of they
rosin and parrofin and take a cleane panne and put therein thy
oyle and thy rosin and parrofin seethe them on a fire of Charcoale
that is soft and easie that it may boyle easiely and not hastily
and as soon as thy rosin and paroofin is molten and relented
and well medled with thyne oyle then sett it downe of the fire
and lett it coole and then vse it when thou think It good./.

To make a perfect water./.

Take Betany avence red mint wild mint Saxifrag noseblede
Spragum Burst Ambrose puliolmontium hillwort prim
=rose, Scabiose, mustard the red nettle, Sotherwood rosemary
woodroofe Masidony    the floes of Borage parsley pellett
hysope Sauory fetherfoy An 5/3 4. and lett them bee putt in a
fuse of stronge wine of Osey or of the best bastard by half a
day naturam from thine foorth./.

The vertue of this water is this.
In primis it gladdeth and comforteth the sight and the memory
of man it comforteth the braine and the full stomacke and it
maketh







maketh gladd the spirituall things gathered or strangled in
the stomacke it purgeth ill humours in workeing and in bringing
other good and it moueth and asswageth sorrowe of the head
and of the teeth.    and if one spoonfull bee drunken
and vsed early and late before his going forth by the space of
15 dayes it will doe away all feuers alsoe it shall heale
maruelouslie the shaking palsie in the tongue it taketh
away the Collicke passion with the gripeing of the wombe
or the sorrow of the raines it is a good medicine for venome
taken or the stinging of a serpent or any other venomous
beast it profitteth much the stranguilion as well of the
raines as of the wombe, for ould stinking in the teeth it is
good for hard hearing and deafnes if a little bee put wett
in the eare if it bee taken with the iuyce of a plantane or
2 spoonfull early and late healeth the emerods; Alsoe it endeth
Fairely the secrett passions of a woman, alsoe it is good for
a sore called Noli me tangere or for all wounds or veines
or cutt synnewes and loset a sunder with a lynnen cloth made
wett and put thereto and euery day fasting one spoonfull taken
in his mouth and take 2 or 3 spoonfulls early and late and it
preserueth a mans body in health maruelouslie./.

A remedy for eyes that bee sore and the lydds
turned ouer beneath./
Take arnamint and honey and the white of an egge of all a like
much and temper them together and take heards and wett
them and wringe out the water and doe these 3 things
vpon the heards as it weare a plaster and lay it thereon and
of any ill blood or water bee therein it shall draw it out./.

A remedy for runing eyes./.
Take a red coale leafe and annoynt it with the gleare of
an egge and lay it on thine eye when thou goest to bedd./

A remedy for wormes that eate the eylidds./.
Take the Calamint and burne it and quench it with white
wine



wine then burne it and quench it againe and doe this 9 times
then mayst thou keepe this Calamint all the yeare and when thou
hast done thus therewith, take as much as a beane and grinde
it vpon a mold and temper it with an egg shell full of white
wine, and lett it rest and wett a fether in the shell and annoynt
thine eyes therewith when thou goest to bedd and doe thus three
nights and it shall kill the wormes and clense thine eyes./.

To knowe whether the man or the woman bee
the cause of barreness./.
Take 2 or 3 kernells of barly and put one kernell in one
vessell and another kernell in another vessell, Then lett the
man pisse vpon one kernell and the woman vpon another, then
put them in a vessell and in a secrett place by 8 dayes and the
fault is where noe fruite cometh that is in those kernells that springeth not./.

A conceit to cause all the ratts and mise in a house
to come together in one place./.
Take one ot two ratts or mice and put them into a pott broken
with holes and stop it well soe that the mise cannott gett out
and after that make a fire about the pott a little from  and
when they shall feele the heate of the fire they shall cry then
as many as heeareth them shall come to thine

To cure the morphew







Take imi StandSeede and salt and stampe them together and
temper them with vinegar and annoynt therewith the morphew

Another
Take blacke sope and vinegar and quicke brim stone by even portions
and medle them together and annoynt ther therewith and bee whole.

Another for the same
Take greene Coperas and put it in cleane water and lett it stand
a night or a day and after clense it and annoynt ther therewith.

To knowe whether the mophew be incurable or not.
Take a needle and pricke the place where the morfew is, soe
that the point of the needle passe not the thicknes of the skyn
with in the flesh and if the blood come out after the pricking
it is curable els not.

Another
Take a stiff cloth and rub the morfewe and if it looks redd
it is curable els not.

Another
If the morphew bee broken and have in any spotts it is hard to heale it.

A remedy for wormes that eate the ey lidds.
Take salt and burne it in a lymen cloth and after temper
it with honey and with a feather annoynt the ey lidds when
thou goest to bed.

A remedy for tender eyes
Take the iuyce of plantaine with the white of an egge and wett
therein a little flaxe and lay it upon thine eyes when thou goest
to bedd.

To knowe whether the disease bee hott or could
Take roses and seeth them in water and lay them in a cloth
and lay it on the eyes and if the sore come of heate hee shall feele
more paine and if it come of could hee shall have lesse paine.

To know whether a goute bee hott or could.

Take vinegar and lye meale and madle them together and
lay on the goute and if the goute bee hott it shall nott
grieve the patient and if it bee could it shall greve the patient
much.

A remedy for the could goute
Take cowslips sage lavander and primrose and seeth them in
runing water and wash the sore therewith.

Another
Take oyle de bay and heate it over the fire and put it in woole
and lay it to the sore and it helpeth.

A remedy for the hott goute
A annoynt the goute with the oyle of henbane and this is a
cheefe remedy for the mygrin.

Another
Take the marrowe of a horse legge and medle it with oates
over the fire and lay it on woole which is cliped of a sheepe
and lay it thereto.

A remedy for all manner of Goutes.
Take a peece of lether or of lynnen cloth and heate it well at

goute and if hee bee curable this shall helpe.

Another
Take the marrowe of the right legge of a horse and may
butter and medle them well together and annoynt the sore
therewith.

Another
Take moleyum and seeth it in wine and it helpeth the hott goute
and seeth it in water and it helpeth the could goute.

For Chilblanes and the itch

Take the seedes of orabus and boyle them in water and wash
thy hands and body.

Another for the itch

Take the rootes of red dockes and sorrell and boyle them in
water and wash your body therewith.

Another for the same

Bray the tops and heads of popie and mixe them with vinegar
and soe annoynt the place or els dissolue aloes with endiue
water and it will do the like.

For ach and swelling

make a seare cloth and hott lay it to the ach or swelling

For the swelling or synewes that are shrunke

Take a pottle of wormes and wash all the dirt out of them
put a pinte of whitewine amongst them and seeth them
together a little while then putt in a pinte of sallett oyle and
lett them seethe till all the wine be consumed away then
streyne them, and sett them on the fire againe then putt
them in 2 ounces of deere sewett and 2 ounces of sheepe
sewett and an ounce of waxe melt all together and keep
it in a glasse annoynt the sore therewith.

Water soith the Plague

Take a lb of selondine jlb of rosemary sage jlb balme jlb
mugworth jlb wormewood jlb rewe jlb Savions jlb dragons
jlb pimpernell jlb egrimony, and lett them dry one day
then put them in a close pott and put thereto 2 gallons of
whitewine lett them soke 4 dayes and 4 nights together
then still them and put them in glasses and giue the sicke
x spoonefull to drinke bloodwarme soe soone as hee is
greeued and lett him walke a little after if hee bee able
or else helpe him and giue it him before sleepe and lett him
neither eate nor drinke in two howers after the elder the
water is the better it is this water is good alsoe for the
quartane ague or any other disease that lyeth at the heart
or about the stomacke

For the stomacke
Take the suger of plantane and bartis ground and fry it hott

To make one that is deafe to heare
Take a yonge ash that groweth by it selfe and cut it in
small pesces then make a fire with & hartoake and lay the
ashes over the fire and save the sap that cometh out of the
end in a sawer and then put it warmes to the eares abd stio
the eares with blackewoole and within 3 dressings hee shall
have his hearing.

For the Cough and Stitch.

Take a grate onyon and cut of the top, take out the coare
and fill it full of Triacle and put in as much peper

drinke of it moreneing and euening and and annoynt his side
with May butter aquavite and a peece of rose cake
melted together.

For the toothache

Take a little honey and bruised peper and the white of an

Take and drinke 4 dayes first and last take

For a sore mouth.

Take a handfull of sage and as much rosemary and seeth it

For a swelling.

Take a pinte of vinegar and halfe a pint of water, boyle it hott
vpon a chafeing dish of coales and put therein 2 or 3 Lynnen
clothes and soe bath the swelling with the clothes as hott as
may bee euer changing them; when one is could then take
another and wring it and lay it to hott and soe doe a quarter
of an hower together and then annoynt the sore with the
oyle aforesaid which is for the swelling.

For a wett in the eye.

Take the gall of a Calge and dry it in the sunne and make
powder thereof and put it in the eye.

For bruiseing of bones.

Take mallowes wormewood sage rew, and seeth all these
together in faire water, then take temrale and boares greace
and cow milke and mingle all these together then boyle it
till it bee thicke and all hott lay it to the sore.

For the ague

Take bursa pastoris and bruise it in a mortar a little and
and put it in a paire of Lynnen sockes and put them on the
patients ffeete when hee goeth to bed and vse them.

For a fistula

Take a gallon of runing water and a handfull of Celondine

Take a pounde of orange pills and lay them to socke 4 dayes

Aegiptiacum how To make Ipsiacum

Take a pounde of honey Ilb of vinegar 5 5 ounces of verdigrece
and lett it seeth together till it bee thicke. first vi: & hony
then put in Ver: in pouder

To lay one of the pockes

Take 2 ounces of Ceruse and bruise it smale then put it
in 2 ounces of oyle debay and stirre them both together
untill it bee white and then put in 4 oz of porkes greace
and stirre them together that one bee not perceaued by the
other, and then put in 2 oz of quickesiluer but kill it first
and stirre it and make it looke on an ash collour and soe
to take a quantitie of a walnutt vnto euery ioynt chafeing
it by the fire &c: then to lye vj howers in his bed sweating
this vse daylie as long as your recait lasts

For a woman that hath a greate belly

Take polipodium that growth vpon an oke as much as will
stuffe a yong pullett and seeth them ni two gallons of faire
water then put to an ounce of any seede, an ounce of Liqurice
Lett them boyle together vntill it come to a quarte and drinke
it first and last.

For a man that hath a greate belly

Take and beate an oz. of rubarbe in fine powder and halfe an oz
of diagragis, and beate it into powder and an oz of Cynamon
2 of sugar 2 oz of ginger and mingle all these together giue
him to drinke as much as will lye vpon a shilling

Mundificatiue

Take the iuyce of smallage and of plantane and make salve

fine flower and 2 yolkes of egs a little honey

For the drynesse withe hands

Take oates and risty bacon and red coaleworts and seeth them
all together till the oates burst and then bath your hands
ouer it vntill it bee could.

For a burnt yard

Take verdegreace 1 oz. and a quarte of white wine and seeth
all vntill it come to a pinte and therewith spoute the yarde.

For the Fumes

Take masticke 1 oz. olibanum 1 oz. antemunicum 1 oz. stora
Calamintha 1 oz. begiang 1 oz. senabryome 1 oz. and beate them
all to powder.

For burneing and scalding.

Take egges and seeth them as hard as you can and then take
the yelkes of them and fry them till you see an oyle come
from them then annoynt the sore.

Diett Drinke

Take a pound of gnaicum and steepe it ouer night in a gallon
of water and in the morneing seeth it a little on the fire and
put in 2 oz of Sirrupe Syney and 2 oz of polipodie and 2 oz of
any seedes and a sticke of Liquerice and seeth it till it come
to a pottle then straine it.

For the Strangury

Take a pinte of feinetary water or white wine and halfe
a pinte of slowes and seeth them together vntill it come to
halfe a pinte put thereto a little sugar and drinke it mornenig
an euening

For a stripe in the eye

Take a quarte of white wine a quarte of dill water and halfe
a quarter of an ounce of Camphire beaten very smale then put
them all in a platter and sett it vpon a Chafing dish of coales
and couer it ouer with another platter and the dew which is
vpon the vpper platterput in a glasse and that putt in the
eye





eye put thereto Cowslipe water.

For the headach

Make vervine lye, or lye of Betany or of fumitary or of
wormewoode and therewith wash they head thrice a weeke

A clenseing of the heade

Take and Chewe the roote of pellitory 3 dayes and it shall clense
thy head and put away the akeing and fasten thy teeth.

A drinke for the head.

Take Betany verveine wormewood Celondine Planten Rue
walwort sage and 2 peper cornes stampe them and seeth them
together in water and drinke it fasting

An oyntment for the head

Take the iuyce of walwort and waxe and stirre them together
ouer the fire and annoynt thy head therewith.

For ache of the eares

Take mustard seede and rewe and stampe them together and
temper it with water and lay it to thy head.

For deafenesse

Take greene bowes of hasill and put one end of them in the
fire and keepe the water that cometh out of the other
end and mingle that water with the iuyce of Leekes heads
and put thereof in thy whole eare and lye vpon the other
eare and doe thus often, Alsoe take the greace that dropeth
from an rosted eele and mingle it with the iuyce of housleeke
heads of both take a like much and doe there with as with
the othere.

For Feeble sight and for eyes that be redd

Take white gingifer that is a manner of sauce and rub it
on a whetstone into a faire bason and take a portion of salt
and enfrase and stampe theme together and put the iuyce
thereof to the gingifer and lett it stand soe all night and
take that on the morrowe that fleeteth aboue and keepe
it in a glasse and annoynt thy eyes there with when thou goest
to bed and when thou risest.



130
121

For eyes that bee runinge

Take a leafe and annoynt it with and lay it to thine
eyes when thou goest to bedd.

For to take away the web of thy eyes

Take a good quantitie of enfrase and stampe it and wringe the
iuyce through a cloth and take swines greace henns greace and
goose greace of each a like much, and melt it together and put
the iuyce thereto and put it in a boxe and therewith annoynt
thine eyes when thou goest to bedd.

For to kill wormes that eate the eyelidds

Take salt, burne it, and stampe it put honey thereto and lay to
thy eylidds when thou goest to bed.

For soreness of the throate or of the mouth

Take olyfe and seeth it in water and when it is well sodden hould
thy mouth ouer it that thou may take the smell and stuff well
thy head with it and after sup one sup of the water white it
is hott and hould it in thy mouth till it bee could doe soe often
3 dayes together.

For loosenig of speeche
Take the iuyce of soche or of primrose and put it in his
mouth.

For spitting of blood

Take Betany and stampe it with water and drinke it fastnig
and drinke it fasting and if thou haue drunke venome or poyson
that day thou shalt cast itvp.

For poyson

If thou haue drunke poyson take Betany and dry it and make
powder thereof and take there as much as thou mayest hould
betweene thy 5 fingers at twice and seeth it in 3 cups of wine
till the third parte bee sodden in and drinke it fasting.

To make a man vomitt or laxatiue

Take the powder of Lorrell and put thereto a spoonefull or two
of honey.





For a stinking breath and nose.

Take black night and the thriuyes from of both a like
and put it into thy nostril.
For toothache of worms
Take henban seed leek seed and score and lay these 3 in
in a mohottful stone and take a pipe that is wide breath
and narrow above and set the small end to thy tooth and the
other on the stone that the smoke may come to the toothe
Another
Take oatmeal and lay it in wine that it is thick and put
it in a lymen cloth in maner of a plaster and lay there to.
After take paper and trinper it with wine that is hott and hard
them of in they mouth till it for -- and for
thrics, After take harts horns and the red sirnell and suther
ashes in a lymen cloth and smift to the sun tooth and it shall
fasen the tooth.
For worms of the teeth
Take the rock of henban and rub their of a little seeds and
lay it to thy teeth and it shall heal the worms within 3 days
and 3 nights.
For greuanes of the hart and loosing of appetite
to meals
Take cenory and teeth it eill in stafral and when it is will
Lossen stamps it will and thorn thens through a cloth and
take the one part of the inyes and 2 of honey and teeth thone
well together and up this falting and it shall put away glatt
from the hart and deeling of appetite to meals.
For A snill stomach
Take arh seeds lyn seed and tumin and stamps thore and
give it to drink with hott water.
For snolling of the stomache.
Take the root of morch and the roots of finisell poino
then and temper it with wriz and it help it.
For the mummiform
Take deans fron and heat it till it lerd hott and put it
in poeet milke and give him to drinke.
For wounds
Take sroias grass and melt it and take honey wris and Rye
meal and teeth it together and lay it upon a cloth on  the
wounds and it shall clensse and heale, and of the wounds gos
logs the take wilde neps and make a plaftes there of and
Lay on the wounds and it shall open it.
To heal the wounde
Take the pooder of Centory and stroroe it on the wounde.
Broken Bones
Diolett drunken will caft out broken bones out of the wounds.
A good plafter for boyles
Take the yolks of eggs and minigls them together with flaxe.
and lay it on the sore.
To know whether a wounded man shall
live or dye.
Take pimpernell and pound it and temper it with water
and give him to drinke and if hretalf thee shall dye.
To know whether hes that halth the bloody
Mensfon shall live or dye
Taake a of Conker seeds and give it him to eate and lett
him drink after it redronis or water and loe for 3 days
and if it stanch her shall live or if hee shall dye.
For warts.
Take Egrmony starmps it and tremper it with oyle and
leave it to thy wart.
For aches of brifed bloode of wounds or of boyle
Take the fatt baron of an outs sronie and melt it and sorlet
it stands till the salt be gone to the bottoms. Take when the
clear that is a core and put it in a pam and put as much
waxs and pitch there to and when they for ------
put there to form and stir it well together till it ----
and for keeps it.





For ache and swelling that turne to byles.

Take lynseede, wheate, holy okes, and sheepes tallowe stampe them
together heate it well and put it in a cloth and binde it to the
sore and it shall shrinke away and become whole.

A good oyntment for wounds.

Calauance 67 oz. oyle pigill and savigle ache and herb Robart lb j
water wrybred ribworst daysies, the Crop of red wort, crop of the
red bryer hollyocks stampe each one by it selfe and take of each one
a like quantitie of iuyce, and put it in a panne; the hollyokes
is soe fatt that you may haue noe iuyce there of. therefore cast them
in all together; take then vergins waxe and honey, fresh sheeps
tallowe, May butter and wine of each a like much and swines
greace alsoe, and looke that all these things bee halfe soe much as
the iuyce of the herbs and seeth all these together till it bee
sodden greene and the hollyockes leaues bee soft then cast score
there to and stirre it well together then clense it through a
cloth and put it in a vessell to keepe.

For men that cannot hould their water

Take goats tallowe burne it and make there with powder eate the
powder in pottage.

For the dropsie when water is betweene the skyn
and the Flesh.

Take the shauenigs of sheeps skynnes and sett them in water till
they bee as thicke as glewe put in a cloth and binde it to his body.

For the feuer quotidiane

Take merche seede and make thereof powder and put a little
thereof in a spoonefull of could water, and giue him to drinke
when you thinke the fitt will take him.

For the feuer tertian.

Make a Cake of drake meale and before it come lett him eate
there of as much as hee may and drinke goodwine thereto good
and plenty take alsoe 4 plants of weybred with the roote and
wash and pound them temper the iuyce with one spoonefull of
honey of wine and 4 of water and giue him that to drinke before the
aooesee of the fever.

For scabbs and burnings.
Take swines greace melt it and yolkes of eggs therewith and
meddle them well together and therewith smere the sore euening
and morneing and lay thereto a coalewort leafe till it be whole-
or take docke leaues and lay on the sore and renewe the leaues -
till it bee whole.

For wounds when bones bee not broken.

Take the roote of Madder and tansey the crop in summer and
the roote in winter the Crop of the red nettle but take as much
of madder as of all the rest, stampe them and temper them
with wine of ould ale and giue him to drinke; Lay a red colerwort
leafe to the wound till it bee whole.

For the gowte fester.

The gowte fester is knowne from the Canker in this wise; the
fester hath a mary hole within and many holes without at
once the Canker hath but one. for festers make this plaster
Take wheate burne it and make powder there of and put in
the hole as deep as you may. and plaster abouean oxe turde

For wounds of the head or other plaes of a man that
they fester not.

Make a drinke of bugill and pgill savigle herb robart an ounce
crop of Caule red, crop of the nettle Crop of red bryer tansey
the roote of Madder empatory burnt of each a like quantity
but of madder as much as of all the rest, and if you dare
search the wound, take the red Caule tansey hemp horse mynt
Crop of the red nettle and as much of madder as of all the rest
stampe them together and make thereof round balls as it
weare a nutt and lett him drinke them or eate them or els
drinke the iuyce with stale ale and if it come out of the
wound it is a signe of life and if hee cast it out of his mouth
it is a signe of death, and if it comes to the wounde s[..]rth the
wound and saue the broken bones that you tame not the
reme of the braine, and if it bleede fast wise it [.]oay softly

Take a peliole reble bone roote piliole, marbeine, ambrose, ribroort

For a red blenies in a mans face

Take the roote of Plantane and the roote of taseells and salt
and stampe then together and bemfin it with eysell and -
therewith wash your face.

For swelling brests in men or women

Take driest of oysell and vergins waxe and make a plaster and
lay it hott to the sore.

For holes in the brests.

Burne a sheepes eye to powder in a newe earthen pott and
put thereof in the hole.



133
124

For gowte of festere

Take Rye flower and temper it with rootes of mallowes
and make thereof little Cakes take the pith and binde it to the
sore hott. and when it could renewe it.

For the gowte fester with many holes

Take the iuyce of ribwort and as much of the white of an
egge and Rye meale and make thereof a plaster and lay thereto
and lett it lye soe till it fall away it selfe. doe soe alwayes till
it bee whole.

For ache in the backe.

Take Rosewater vinegar and womans milke and wett a cloth
therein and lay it to the backe.

Finis.
